# 2026-10-29 · #90 *Tyto furcata pratincola* v4 — documented P20 standing, down-dominant stage

## ABO-WAGNER2022-P20

- **Stable source ID:** `ABO-WAGNER2022-P20`
- **Title / authors / year:** “Development of the horizontal optocollic reflex in juvenile barn owls (*Tyto furcata pratincola*)”; Hermann Wagner, Ina Pappe, Sandra Brill et al.; 2022.
- **DOI / original URL:** https://doi.org/10.1007/s00359-022-01555-0 ; open full text https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00359-022-01555-0
- **Accessed:** 2026-10-29.
- **Source type:** peer-reviewed open-access primary developmental study; Journal of Comparative Physiology A 208:479–492.
- **Taxon / stage:** exact North American subspecies *T. f. pratincola*; six hand-raised young, P9–P65.
- **Actually read / viewed:** full article Introduction and Methods/General observations, lines 79–102 and 145–161; Figure 1 full-size photo sequence directly viewed.
- **Source facts:** eyes open around P10–12; thermal support is needed to about P15; young can stand/walk around P20; exploration increases after P30; flight begins around P60 and adult feather length around P67. Figure 1 shows P20 still strongly white-down-dominant with emergent dark wing feathers and an upright but low-supported stance.
- **Limits / conflicts:** the six birds were hand-raised and the paper’s main endpoint was visual reflex, not wild nestling plumage. Timing cannot be universalised to every population or individual; Figure 1 is a stage series, not a quantified feather map.
- **Design / review decision:** v4 deliberately replaces v1–v3’s late fully feathered juvenile with a P20 standing/walking juvenile: abundant white down, short emergent dark wing feathers, short/hidden tail and both feet supported on a flat nest-box floor. No branch perch or flight.
- **Links:** [species card](../knowledge/species/90-tyto-furcata.md) · [v4 target](../design/concepts/90-tyto-furcata-v4.jpg) · [run](../production/runs/202610291458+0800-cycle-6.md)

## ABO-PHILLIPS2022-GROWTH

- **Stable source ID:** `ABO-PHILLIPS2022-GROWTH`
- **Title / author / year:** “Developmental Growth Rates of American Barn Owl (*Tyto furcata*) Nestlings and Comparison of Sexes”; Emily Phillips; 2022.
- **Original URL:** https://escholarship.org/uc/item/4d0278kp
- **Accessed:** 2026-10-29.
- **Source type:** University of California thesis record/primary morphometric study; abstract available, full item embargoed until 9 December 2028.
- **Taxon / stage:** American Barn Owl nestlings/fledglings in California’s Central Valley; males and females.
- **Actually read:** complete repository abstract and metadata.
- **Source facts:** male and female fledged nestlings showed similar overall growth patterns; hallux, culmen and body mass at fledging had not reached adult values; the study concludes growth continues after fledging.
- **Limits / conflicts:** no dataset/full text is available during the embargo and the abstract gives no P20 feather colours or fixed sex ratio. It cannot calibrate the exact size difference of a depicted pair.
- **Design / review decision:** use as a growth/sex caution only: the P20 juvenile must not be a miniature adult, and adult female direction may be fuller/darker without forcing a universal proportional height gap.
- **Links:** [species card](../knowledge/species/90-tyto-furcata.md) · [run](../production/runs/202610291458+0800-cycle-6.md)

## Reused evidence, taxonomy and corrected inference

- **FUR-CORNELL-CAM2010:** Texas nest-camera synthesis reused for nest-box context, darker/buffer/more spotted female tendency, paler male, and the long 35–65-day flight-feather development window.
- **FUR-MIK2013-82:** printed pp.82–84 / PDF pp.84–86 previously read; reused for whitish facial disc with orange-brown rim, dark eyes, creamy bill, pale feathered legs/toes and *pratincola* adult colour direction.
- **Current / historical name:** current project list uses *Tyto furcata* (subspecies *T. f. pratincola*); older literature frequently uses *Tyto alba pratincola*. This v4 does not merge the American taxon with project #01 European *T. alba*.
- **Corrected design inference:** v1–v3 chose a late, nearly adult-feathered juvenile. V4 selects the directly pictured P20 locomotor boundary so stage-specific down, wing length and support can be inspected. “Can stand/walk” does not mean branch-perching or flight.
- **Unknown:** individual sex, exact day, wild-vs-hand-raised timing shift, precise feather-tract sequence, toe geometry and an individual adult pair’s ratio. Science remains false. The user-uploaded Mikkola file was unavailable in this runtime; only the already indexed page notes are reused.

## Full image-generation prompt

Create a landscape 3:2 natural-history concept plate in the established warm painterly-realistic owl style, titled exactly “AMERICAN BARN OWL” with italic subtitle “Tyto furcata pratincola”. Show exactly three birds left-to-right with exact labels “ADULT MALE”, “~20-DAY JUVENILE”, “ADULT FEMALE”. Setting: a clean but natural wooden Texas ranch nest-box/loft opening with broad flat floor, sparse pellets/dry grass and open grassland light beyond; no woven nest, eggs, prey, humans, UI, border or extra birds.

Adults: heart-shaped whitish facial discs with warm orange-brown rim, dark eyes, creamy horn bills, gold-grey mottled upperparts. Male very pale/white below with sparse spots; female modestly fuller/darker/buffer and more spotted, but height ranges overlap and the display difference must remain subtle. Pale feathered legs/toes, complete natural talons, secure two-foot support.

Center juvenile: specifically around post-hatching day 20, when *T. f. pratincola* can just stand/walk but is nowhere near flight. It is smaller, low/upright with both feet fully supported on the flat box floor; body overwhelmingly wrapped in loose white natal down, rounded head without a finished adult heart-shaped disc, open dark eyes and small pale bill. Wings are only short side stubs with a few emerging dark brown quills/short juvenile feathers, not long folded adult wings; tail absent or hidden. Do not show branch perching, wing-spread, flight or adult-regular spot rows. Exactly three birds; all text legible and exact.

# 2026-10-29 · #89 *Bubo bengalensis* v3 — c.35-day full-juvenile-plumage, pre-flight-inference boundary

## BEN-PANDE-DAHANUKAR2011

- **Stable source ID:** `BEN-PANDE-DAHANUKAR2011`
- **Title / authors / year:** “Ecological effects on morphometric development of the Indian Eagle Owl *Bubo bengalensis*”; Satish Pande & Neelesh Dahanukar; 2011.
- **DOI / original URL:** https://doi.org/10.11609/JoTT.o2609.1677-85 ; author-uploaded article page read at https://www.researchgate.net/publication/230777101_Ecological_effects_on_morphometric_development_of_the_Indian_Eagle_Owl_Bubo_bengalensis
- **Accessed:** 2026-10-29.
- **Source type:** peer-reviewed primary morphometric study; Journal of Threatened Taxa 3(4):1677–1685.
- **Taxon / stage:** *Bubo bengalensis*; 16 young aged 1–58 days.
- **Actually read:** complete abstract and article metadata on the author-uploaded page; variables, sample and conclusions were checked against the journal citation.
- **Source facts:** wing, tarsus, claw, beak, tail and body mass were measured across 1–58 days. Growth of characters was loosely coupled, and the authors reported geographical/environmental developmental plasticity.
- **Limits / conflicts:** the accessible text did not expose a day-by-day colour/feather plate; n=16 and local ecology do not justify a universal flight day. Morphometric development cannot be inferred from “fully feathered” alone.
- **Design / review decision:** v3 may use the independent 35-day plumage observation from BEN-RAM2009, but must not turn that into a flight claim or mature adult proportions; keep a grounded/crouched bird with a shorter juvenile tail and folded wings.

## BEN-RAMANUJAM2015-BEHAVIOR

- **Stable source ID:** `BEN-RAMANUJAM2015-BEHAVIOR`
- **Title / author / year:** “Time Budget and Behavioral Pattern of the Indian Eagle Owl *Bubo bengalensis* during the Breeding Season”; M. E. Ramanujam; 2015.
- **Original URL:** https://www.researchgate.net/publication/283089932_Time_Budget_and_Behavioral_Pattern_of_the_Indian_Eagle_Owl_Bubo_bengalensis_during_the_Breeding_Season
- **Accessed:** 2026-10-29.
- **Source type:** primary focal-family behavioural study; author-uploaded article/abstract.
- **Taxon / stage:** one *B. bengalensis* family at the nest, January–March 2011; dependent young.
- **Actually read:** complete abstract and full-text excerpts on the author page, including young-bird activity and nest-leaving descriptions.
- **Source facts:** young spent most observed time resting or moving; feeding, pellet casting and wing flapping were less frequent. They became more active after about 10 days and gradually moved outside the immediate nest area at about 20 days; the account reports self-feeding on delivered prey while still dependent.
- **Limits / conflicts:** one family and behavioural observations do not define plumage for every day or prove sustained flight; “left the nest” is not synonymous with fledging/flight.
- **Design / review decision:** use only to diversify the c.35-day juvenile into a natural low resting/crouched pose on a supported mineral ledge. Do not show flight, aerial wing exercise or unsupported branch perching.
- **Links:** [species card](../knowledge/species/89-bubo-bengalensis.md) · [v3 concept](../design/concepts/89-bubo-bengalensis-v3.jpg) · [run](../production/runs/20261029T1446+0800-cycle-5.md)

## Reused evidence and corrected inference

- **BEN-RAM2009:** full primary PDF re-read, especially printed pp.519–521 and plate sequence. It documents whitish natal down and closed eyes at 0–5 days, pin sheaths/eyes open at 7 days, onset of prejuvenile moult around 14–15 days, and full juvenile plumage by about 35 days, persisting beyond 60 days. Its Puducherry sample is preliminary; the paper warns against broad generalisation.
- **BEN-MIK2013-274:** adult boundary reused: orange-yellow eyes, greenish-horn/slate-black bill, brown ear tufts/dark crown, tawny-brown black-mottled upperparts, small upper-breast streaks and fine/faint lower crossbars. Female wing/tail measurements are longer, but sexed masses are unavailable; do not state “female heavier.”
- **Corrected design inference:** v1/v2 used the well-supported neonatal stage. v3 deliberately changes to the separately documented c.35-day full-juvenile-plumage stage. “Full juvenile plumage” is not “adult plumage” and not proof of flight ability; growth variables are decoupled.
- **Unknown:** exact individual hatch date, sex, subspecies/local morph, colour calibration, and a quantified first-flight threshold. Science remains false. The user-uploaded Heimo Mikkola file was unavailable in this runtime; no page citation is invented.

## Full image-generation prompt

Create a landscape 3:2 natural-history concept plate in the repository’s established warm, painterly-realistic wildlife style, titled “INDIAN EAGLE-OWL” with italic subtitle “Bubo bengalensis”. Show exactly three birds, left-to-right, with exact bottom labels “ADULT MALE”, “~35-DAY JUVENILE”, “ADULT FEMALE”. Place them in a rocky semi-desert ravine at warm dawn/dusk, with mineral ledges, sparse dry scrub and natural contact shadows; no frame, UI, scale bars, prey, eggs or extra birds.

Adults: near-equal visible body height; do not make the female categorically heavier. The female may have a subtly longer closed wing/tail only. Both have orange-yellow irises, a greenish-horn to slate-black bill, prominent brown ear tufts, dark crown, white brows and blackish facial ruff; tawny-brown upperparts mottled black; small narrow dark upper-breast streaks and fine faint lower-belly crossbars, not a solid dark Cape-Eagle-Owl breastplate. Feathered reddish-yellow tarsi/toes with only cautiously suggested greyer bare outer joints and dark claws. Both feet naturally supported.

Centre juvenile: explicitly approximately 35 days, at the documented full juvenile plumage stage, not a neonatal chick and not a miniature adult. Rounded juvenile proportions, softer buff-grey/tawny juvenile contour plumage, folded developing wings with complete juvenile cover but no exaggerated adult-hard regular barring, shorter tail, residual softer texture around head/shoulders, orange-yellow opening-to-bright eyes and dark small bill. Resting/crouched low on a broad mineral ledge with both feet/support visible; wings closed, no airborne pose, no claim of sustained flight. Keep clear age contrast while avoiding unsupported exact feather-count or sex claims.

# 2026-10-29 · #88 *Bubo capensis* v4 — approximately 45-day pre-flight nest-leaving boundary

## CAP-OGADA-KIBUTHU2012

- **Stable source ID:** `CAP-OGADA-KIBUTHU2012`
- **Title / authors / year:** “Breeding Ecology of Mackinder’s Eagle-Owls (*Bubo capensis mackinderi*) in Farmlands of Central Kenya”; Darcy L. Ogada & Paul M. Kibuthu; 2012.
- **DOI / original URL:** https://doi.org/10.3356/JRR-11-90.1 ; author-uploaded full text read at https://www.researchgate.net/publication/257412835_Breeding_Ecology_of_Mackinder%27s_Eagle-Owls_Bubo_capensis_mackinderi_In_Farmlands_of_Central_Kenya
- **Accessed:** 2026-10-29.
- **Source type:** peer-reviewed primary five-year field study; Journal of Raptor Research 46(4):327–335.
- **Taxon / stage:** *B. c. mackinderi* only; nests, nestlings, operational fledging classification.
- **Actually read:** full-text Methods pp.328–329 and Results pp.329–330, plus abstract.
- **Source facts:** the study followed 13 pairs/39 breeding attempts; 65% of nests were in caves or on covered ledges. Hatching dates were estimated from nestling size; the authors operationally classified young as fledged at approximately 70 days and then confirmed them by later territory observations. Nests were also recorded on open ledges or ground.
- **Limits / conflicts:** this is the larger East African subspecies *mackinderi*, not the nominate South African concept used for adult plumage. The ~70-day value is the study’s classification rule, not a universal measured first-flight threshold; it provides no detailed nestling plumage sequence.
- **Design / review decision:** use only as a cross-check that a ~45-day bird should not be shown in sustained flight and that cave/ledge support is biologically grounded. Do not transfer *mackinderi* adult colour/size to v4.
- **Links:** [species card](../knowledge/species/88-bubo-capensis.md) · [v4 image](../design/concepts/88-bubo-capensis-v4.jpg) · [run](../production/runs/20261029T1437+0800-cycle-4.md)

# 2026-10-29 · #62 *Ketupa sumatrana* v3 — late dependent juvenile boundary

## BEOW-SGBIRDS-RAPTOR-2021

- **Title / institution / date:** “Singapore Raptor Report, Late Spring Migration, April–June 2021”; Singapore Bird Group / Nature Society (Singapore); 11 July 2021.
- **Original URL:** https://singaporebirdgroup.wordpress.com/2021/07/11/singapore-raptor-report-late-spring-migration-april-june-2021/
- **Accessed:** 2026-10-29 (UTC+08:00).
- **Source type:** regional field-report aggregation with credited photographs; directly opened and read.
- **Species / stage:** *Ketupa sumatrana* under historical *Bubo sumatranus*; recently fledged juvenile.
- **Evidence:** HTML lines 44–47 report a fledgling photographed at Rifle Range Link on 4 April, with two adults at the same locality the previous day. This independently supports branch-capable fledgling presence and nearby adults, not a nest-bound wing-bud stage.
- **Limitations:** no measured age, feather lengths, sex or continuous development series; the report does not alone establish the late 2018 plumage boundary.
- **Decision:** use only as a stage/behavior cross-check; no flight action or sex inference is added.

## Reused source IDs and corrected inference

- **BEOW-SGBIRDS-BREED-2018:** directly re-read. Lines 10–15 give a dated 7–17 August sequence; the juvenile was estimated 8–10 months, almost full-sized, had nearly completed moult to adult-like feathers, retained whitish head/ear tufts, attempted hunting and flew with a snake while an adult remained nearby.
- **BEOW-BESG-JUV-2010:** directly re-read. Lines 18–23 describe a snowy-white juvenile already adult-sized and capable of good flight but unable to feed independently. This is a different dependent stage and must not be blended with the 2018 late-moult bird.
- **BEOW-SGBIRDS-2026:** adult ID boundary and broad juvenile white-with-dark-bars summary.
- **Corrected design inference:** v2's small, white, extensively barred begging juvenile blends the 2010 white adult-sized dependent stage with the 2018 almost-adult late-moult stage. v3 deliberately selects the dated 2018 boundary: almost adult size, adult-like body/wing feathers, whitish head/ear tufts only, closed bill and secure branch support.
- **Unknown:** exact hatch date, continuous moult sequence, quantified sex ratio, juvenile sex and subspecies variation. Science remains false. Heimo Mikkola upload unavailable; no page citation invented.

## Full image-edit prompt

Edit the supplied #62 Barred Eagle-Owl (Ketupa sumatrana) v2 concept in place. Preserve photographic realism, humid Southeast Asian forest/quarry-edge habitat, the title BARRED EAGLE-OWL, italic Ketupa sumatrana, exactly three owls and the three existing bottom labels. Preserve both adults exactly in pose, near-equal visible scale, pitch-black eyes, large yellow bills, very long horizontal outward ear tufts, dark brown-and-buff barred upperparts, blackish-and-whitish finely barred underparts and natural branch support. Make a bounded juvenile-only science revision to the documented August 2018 late dependent stage: enlarge the center juvenile to almost adult size, perched securely on the same branch; most body and wing plumage already adult-like and fully feathered, with mature-looking dark/buff barred folded wings and tail; retain conspicuously whitish head and ear tufts as the remaining juvenile character. Closed bill, alert but non-flight pose. Do not depict a small fluffy nestling, pure natal down, begging gape, shortened wing buds or hidden tail. Do not invent a fixed female-larger rule or sex the juvenile. No explanatory callouts, extra text, prey, eggs, extra birds, UI or border.

- **Source type:** dated local field account with credited photographs; directly opened and read.
- **Species / stage:** *Ketupa ketupu* (page typo “ketupa” noted); Sungei Buloh fern-nest young from 25 March to 25 April 2016.
- **Evidence:** lines 15–25 identify the 25 March photograph as roughly two weeks after discovery, the chick hidden among bird's-nest-fern fronds, and the 25 April bird out on a branch after a short flight. Lines 23–24 describe the nest in a bird's-nest fern on a rain tree and nearby adult sentry perches.
- **Limitations / conflict:** discovery date is not hatch date; “about two weeks” is not a measured age. This cross-check supports stage ordering and physical support, not precise feather-day calibration or sex-size inference.
- **Design / review decision:** supports a deep, bowl-like living fern with humus/frond-base contact for the early chick; adults may remain nearby but must not be forced into a universal family-pair narrative.

## Reused source IDs

- **BFOW-BESG-NEST-2016:** 25 March chick rested and shifted within a living *Asplenium nidus*; down-covered profile; direct field-photo account.
- **BFOW-NPARKS-2026:** adult identification boundary—long tawny ear tufts, black-streaked buffy-rufous underparts, dark upperparts and yellow iris; forest/mangrove habitat.
- **BFOW-BESG-FLEDGE-2024:** retained only as a later branch-grip/post-fledging comparator; not used to invent the v3 early chick.

## Old-name and inference control

- Current list name: *Ketupa ketupu*; historical combination *Bubo ketupu*; protonym *Strix ketupu*.
- The observed 2016 chick was not sexed. Adult sexes are not forced into a female-larger rule; near-equal visible scale is retained because no species-specific quantified sex ratio was found in this subround.
- Heimo Mikkola upload was not accessible in this runtime; no page citation is invented.

## Full image-edit prompt

Edit the supplied #61 Buffy Fish Owl (Ketupa ketupu) three-bird concept in place. Preserve the established photographic-realism composition, tropical wetland/mangrove atmosphere, living bird's-nest fern nest, water background, title and all three bottom labels. Preserve both adults exactly in identity, pose, scale, warm buff-brown plumage, yellow irises, black bills, lateral tawny ear tufts, heavy dark vertical breast streaks, barred wings/tail, near-equal visible height and bare scaly lower tarsi/toes. Keep exactly three owls: ADULT MALE left, JUVENILE center, ADULT FEMALE right. Make a bounded juvenile-only science correction. The center bird must be an approximately two-week, nest-bound chick documented in the 2016 Sungei Buloh bird's-nest-fern sequence: low resting posture deep between supportive fern-frond bases and trapped humus, rounded body fully dominated by loose white-to-pale-cream natal down, open yellowish eyes and a small dark bill. Remove juvenile ear tufts, adult-like dark wing panels, layered barred remiges, long tail, standing/perching posture and exposed unsupported feet. Wings must read only as short down-covered tucked buds and the tail must be absent or hidden by down and nest material. Keep natural contact shadows and clear physical support. Do not change either adult, do not add birds, prey, eggs, UI, extra labels or decorative borders. Retain legible title “SUNDA BUFFY FISH OWL”, italic “Ketupa ketupu”, and the three exact labels.

## 2026-10-26 #44 Strix uralensis v2 evidence increment

**Verified-source delta:** new 0; reused and freshly read 3 (`UROW-MIKKOLA-2013`, `UROW-LOHMUS-2003`, `UROW-BROMMER-2004`); corrected 0.

Mikkola printed pp.350–352 remain the direct adult/young visual boundary: dark-brown adult eyes, yellow-horn bill, long wedge tail with broad pale bars, strongly longitudinally streaked pale underparts, and a roughly four-week young with a developed facial disc but gray-beige fluffy mesoptile. Lõhmus supports cavities and stick nests with a preference for cavities; Brommer supports a studied nestling period but supplies no drawable feather sequence. Active search also located Eriksson, Lundberg & Westman (1984), *Estimating age of Ural Owl nestlings from body part measurements*, plus a 1987 symposium development figure; direct full-text/figure retrieval failed in this run, so both remain explicitly unverified leads and were not assigned stable source IDs or used for new fine-feather claims. V2 therefore corrects the visible juvenile wing to short soft mesoptile without asserting exact remige geometry or a measured sex-height ratio; `scientifically_verified=false`. **Reuse / uncertainty boundary.** Re-read the complete open primary study `SPOT-PANDE-2011`: eight known-age nestlings from seven intensively studied southern-Indian nests were measured from hatching through 32 days; sexes could not be separated externally; wing growth accelerated after two weeks, reached about 80% of adult chord at 4.5 weeks, and fledging occurred around 4.5 weeks. The authors also observed pre-flight young walking from the nest hole onto branches. For v2, an approximately three-week nestling therefore has a short developing wing, is not shown flying, and remains securely supported at the cavity lip. The paper does not supply a literal feather map; exact remige layering, sex-specific growth and subspecies color variation remain unknown, so `scientifically_verified=false`.

**Reuse / uncertainty boundary.** Reused `SRC-BOOK-037-01` for the 15–17 cm scale, only slight female size difference, greenish-yellow bill/cere, brown/gray/red morphs and the explicit statement that the mesoptile is adult-like but has a plain unspotted crown; reused `SRC-WEB-037-02` for riparian/deciduous forest, dry scrub, gardens and daytime activity. The original description and current institutional pages still do not supply exact juvenile age, iris change, remige/tail sequence or a secure nest-stage photograph. v2 therefore depicts an age-unknown older juvenile with a plain crown and deliberately short, soft, incompletely patterned developing wing/tail; scientific verification remains false.

Juveniles have shorter tails and much-reduced white spotting on head/back, with especially obvious false eyes. The new BDI synthesis is explicit that sexes are alike in plumage and that the male is only slightly smaller, so v2 rejects a strongly oversized female. No primary day-by-day juvenile plumage study was found in this cycle; that uncertainty remains open rather than being promoted to scientific verification.

## 2026-10-26 #18 Megascops kennicottii v2 evidence increment

| Stable ID | Title / original URL | Author / institution | Year / accessed | Type | Taxon / stage | Verified evidence | Location | Limits / design use |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| WESO-ADW-HATCH-2026 | [Otus kennicottii (western screech owl)](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Otus_kennicottii/) | Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan Museum of Zoology | page current; accessed 2026-10-26 | Institutional species account citing Cannings & Angell 2001, Cornell 1999, Ehrlich et al. 1988 | *Megascops kennicottii* (syn. *Otus kennicottii*), hatchling | Altricial young hatch with white down, closed eyes and an egg tooth; male provisions female until young are about three weeks old. | Reproduction / Development text | Secondary synthesis rather than direct day-by-day primary measurements. Supports a conservative closed-eye white-down hatchling only; exact age, down density, eye-opening day and feather sequence remain unknown. Links species record 18, concept v2 and run 2026-10-26T0238+0800-cycle-6. Confidence: medium-high for hatch condition. |

Reused:
- WESO-CORNELL-ID-2026: adult yellow eyes, deep bill, small ear tufts, compact square-headed silhouette, bark-like streaking and regional color variation.
- WESO-USFS-NM-2010: adult mass dimorphism in sampled New Mexico populations; mass difference is not converted to standing-height difference.
- Cornell Western Screech-Owl life-history page (accessed 2026-10-26): natural/reused cavities, no constructed nest, eggs laid on material already present; cavity dimensions are descriptive, not a universal template.

Design inference: use near-equal adult displayed height with the female only subtly fuller; select a gray-brown shared morph rather than encoding regional color as sex; depict one closed-eye white-down hatchling low on the natural cavity floor with short down-covered wing buds, concealed tail and full body support. These are evidence-bounded visual decisions, not scientific validation.

## 2026-10-13 · #40 *Glaucidium nubicola*

### SRC-BOOK-040-01
- Title/author/year: *Owls of the World: A Photographic Guide*, Heimo Mikkola, 2013.
- Pages: printed p.378; uploaded PDF page 380, species 181.
- Type/stages: photographic guide; adult morphology, sex direction, habitat; juvenile status.
- Value: 16 cm, 73–80 g, wing 90–96 mm; female wings slightly longer but females are not heavier; round head, no ear tufts, yellow eyes, greenish-yellow bill; practically unspotted warm dark-brown mantle/back, bold pale scapular/upperwing-covert spots, short tail with five incomplete pale-spotted bars, white central throat/breast/belly with rufous-streaked flanks; humid western-Andean cloud forest at 1,400–2,000 m. The guide explicitly states “Juvenile Undescribed.”
- Limitation: no juvenile morphology or day-age; page image rendering returned 403 in this run, so only extracted page text was used. No invented juvenile plumage is supported.
- Supports: adult identification, near-equal adult scale, habitat, and explicit juvenile uncertainty for #40 v1.

### SRC-PAPER-040-02
- Title/authors/year: *A new species of pygmy-owl (Strigidae: Glaucidium) from the Pacific slope of the northern Andes*, Mark B. Robbins & F. Gary Stiles, 1999, The Auk 116:305–315.
- URL: https://www.biologicaldiversity.org/campaigns/global_owl_project/descriptions/Glaucidium/Glaucidium%20nubicola%20%28Robbins%20%26%20Stiles%29%201999%2C%20Auk%2C%20116%2C%20pages%20305-315%20%28front%20cover%29..pdf
- Accessed: 2026-10-13.
- Type/stages: original peer-reviewed species description; adult specimens, voice and range.
- Value: diagnoses the Pacific-slope taxon as distinct and anchors the accepted name, holotype locality/elevation and adult diagnostic comparison.
- Limitation: not a developmental series and does not support juvenile feather chronology.
- Supports: taxonomy and adult diagnostic boundary.

### SRC-PAPER-040-03
- Title/authors/year: *Notes on the breeding of north-west Ecuadorian birds*, Harold F. Greeney & Tony Nunnery, 2006, Bulletin of the British Ornithologists’ Club 126:38–45.
- Accessed: 2026-10-13 through indexed publication citation and breeding excerpt; the primary-paper landing/full-text was not independently rendered in this run.
- Type/stage: original field breeding note; recently fledged juvenile.
- Value: records a recently fledged *G. nubicola* at Tandayapa in August 1999, supporting a mobile, branch-supported fledgling stage and a breeding period extending at least into August.
- Limitation: no directly extracted juvenile plate, exact age, sex, or feather-by-feather description; treated as a stage/behavior boundary only, not a morphology source.
- Supports: use of a conservative, fully supported fledgling pose; does not justify novel plumage detail.

## 2026-10-13 · #41 *Glaucidium cobanense*

### SRC-BOOK-041-01
- Title/author/year: *Owls of the World: A Photographic Guide*, Heimo Mikkola, 2013.
- Pages: printed p.380; uploaded PDF p.382, species 183.
- Type/stages: photographic guide; adult, downy chick and mesoptile.
- Value: 16–18 cm, wing 82–98 mm; no sexual-size data; brown and red morphs, red predominant; yellow eyes, yellowish-horn bill, no ear tufts, false eyes and barred tail. Downy chick whitish; mesoptile adult-like but more diffuse, with grayish unmarked crown and mottled forehead. Mountain forest at higher elevations.
- Limitation: no exact juvenile age or continuous wing/tail development; no sex-size evidence.
- Supports: #41 adult/mesoptile boundaries and explicit near-equal sex scale.

### SRC-PAPER-041-02
- Title/authors/year: *Vocalizations of the Guatemalan Pygmy-Owl (Glaucidium cobanense)*, Knut Eisermann & Steve N. G. Howell, 2011, Journal of Raptor Research 45(4).
- DOI/URL: https://doi.org/10.3356/JRR-10-73.1
- Accessed: 2026-10-13.
- Type: peer-reviewed taxonomic/vocal study.
- Value: documents vocal divergence and the highland distribution used by modern species-level treatments.
- Limitation/conflict: AOS/Clements treatments have historically retained it within *G. gnoma*; this source supports an evidence-based split but does not erase checklist disagreement. No juvenile morphology.

### OWL-SRC-0053-A
- Title: *Strix nebulosa*, Great Gray Owl
- Author/institution: Elena D. Ulev; USDA Forest Service Fire Effects Information System
- Year: 2007
- URL/DOI: https://research.fs.usda.gov/feis/species-reviews/stne ; https://doi.org/10.2737/feis-species-review-stne
- Accessed: 2026-10-13
- Type: institutional literature synthesis
- Species/stage: adult, nestling, recently fledged juvenile
- Useful facts: young leave nests at 26–28.5 days; remain unable to fly for 7–14 days; climb leaning/deeply fissured trees with talons, wings and bill; documents nest sites, habitat and post-fledging support needs.

- Limitations/confidence: high for cited developmental ranges and behavior; synthesized from multiple studies rather than a new primary observation.
- Design decision: preflight juvenile shown low and supported while climbing leaning bark, with no mature flight posture.

### OWL-SRC-0053-B
- Title: Ecology of the Great Gray Owl
- Authors/institution: Evelyn L. Bull and Mark G. Henjum; USDA Forest Service Pacific Northwest Research Station
- Year: 1990
- URL/DOI: https://research.fs.usda.gov/treesearch/5628 ; https://doi.org/10.2737/PNW-GTR-265
- Accessed: 2026-10-13
- Type: primary field study / General Technical Report
- Species/stage: 24 pairs, 107 juveniles, 71 nesting attempts
- Useful facts: nests occurred on stick platforms, broken-off dead-tree tops and artificial platforms; mature/older mixed conifer stands common; recently fledged young need perches and cover.
- Section/pages: abstract; full report 39 pp.
- Limitations/confidence: high for northeastern Oregon population; regional extrapolation is limited.
- Design decision: broken snag/old platform context and nearby leaning-tree support, not a cavity nest.

### OWL-SRC-0053-C
- Title: Great Gray Owl Life History
- Institution: Cornell Lab of Ornithology, All About Birds
- URL: https://www.allaboutbirds.org/guide/great_gray_owl/lifehistory
- Accessed: 2026-10-13
- Type: institutional species account
- Species/stage: egg, hatchling, nestling, adult
- Useful facts: nestling period 26–29 days; hatchlings helpless and down-covered; nests reuse old raptor/raven nests, broken tree tops, mistletoe brooms or platforms, usually without added material.
- Section: Nest Description; Nesting Facts
- Limitations/confidence: high-level synthesis; does not specify the exact down-to-feather sequence for one individual.
- Design decision: supports down-dominant, recently nest-departed stage and non-cavity nesting structure.

## 2026-10-14 · #55 *Bubo lacteus* (current combination *Ketupa lactea*)

### VEO-PF-2026
- Title: Verreaux's Eagle-owl
- Institution: The Peregrine Fund
- URL: https://peregrinefund.org/explore-raptors-species/owls/verreauxs-eagle-owl
- Accessed: 2026-10-14
- Type: raptor-conservation institution species account; adult, nestling and nest-leaving juvenile
- Evidence actually read: lines 17–22, 42–50 and 68–77. Pink eyelids can appear about one week after hatch; adults have dark eyes, creamy dark-edged facial disc, small ear tufts and fine pale vermiculation. Large reused nests and occasional hollows are documented. At about 60 days, young leave the nest before reliable flight, climbing and hopping in the nest tree; first flight follows about two weeks later.
- Limits/confidence: institutional synthesis rather than a primary growth-series paper; age values support behavior, not an exact plumage chronology. High for stage/action and diagnostics, medium for fine juvenile feather detail.
- Design support: branch-supported, non-flying ~60-day climber with down-softened outline and short developing wings; diagnostic pink eyelid allowed without adult feather maturity.

- Limits/confidence: no sex-specific measurements or feather-sequence data. High for identity/habitat, medium for generalized family behavior.
- Design support: dry wooded-savanna setting and restrained family context only.

- Type: accredited-zoo species account, 3-page PDF
- Applies to: adult morphology, sex size, nest setting, juvenile development
- Evidence actually read: PDF p.1 lines 17–35 and p.2 lines 51–60. Orange eyes, prominent ear tufts, tawny mottled brown upperparts, buff lowerparts with black streaks and a whitish throat; males are smaller than females. Rocky scrapes/crevices/cave entrances/sheltered cliff ledges are used. Young begin only short conditioning flights at about 60 days.
- Limits/confidence: institutional synthesis, not a continuous feather-growth series; high for diagnostics and setting, medium-high for the general 60-day behavior boundary.
- Design support: moderate female size increase; supported rocky ledge; ~60-day juvenile remains grounded/perched with short down-softened developing wings and no strong flight pose.

- Evidence actually read: PDF pp.106–107 (file pp.24–25), lines 1981–2074. Juvenile was pale yellow with light spotting, thin V-shaped breast marks, heavily barred dark flight/tail feathers, small ear tufts and creamy-yellow legs; it attempted escape but did not fly far and was judged not yet flying/dependent. Dense moist deciduous forest with semi-evergreen/evergreen stream pockets is documented.

## Uploaded Mikkola2013 document reuse and historical correction · 2026-10-14
Existing WHSO-MIKKOLA-2013 identifies the same uploaded 2nd-edition book; use that document identity rather than duplicating it as six new books. This run successfully read text/captions, not photograph pixels, on these printed pages (PDF pagination +2):
- Tyto soumagnei pp98–99: no sex-size data; reddish-ochre, smoky toes; relatively long tail conflicts with ADW short-tail wording. #65 chosen older stage retained, precise tail length calibration pending.
- Tyto capensis pp104–105: dull yellow/light golden second coat, mainly brown juvenile face/white edges; male wing/tail may be longer, sexed weights absent. Conflicts with broad institution female-larger statement by metric, not resolved; #66 display scale not a measured ratio, mark pending primary study. DOI10.2989/OSTRICH.2008.79.1.11.366 retrieval failed; no primary measurement claimed.
- Pulsatrix perspicillata pp310–311: grey-brown barred mesoptile wing-coverts, brown barred wing/tail; cycle3 all-white wings inappropriate inference, v2 correction in cycle5. Eastern Costa Rica unbarred-belly chapmani visual direction distinguished from western finely barred saturata.
- Tyto tenebricosa pp114–115: usually female heavier but reversed pair reported; whitish/grey-white natal down conflicts with OwlPages sooty-grey wording, avoided by #68 NSW-supported older fledgling. Australian nominate selected, not New Guinea arfaki.
- Tyto novaehollandiae p107: female longer wings; creamy second down generalization, but Debus2007 dark-morph individual buff coat will take priority. Museum white-to-cream fledgling description not applied to all morphs. Tasmanian castanops separate old concept retained, not counted as extra species here.
- Ninox strenua pp450–451: male larger; mesoptile white face/dark eye patches, white sparsely streaked underside, barred wings. Caption young wings/tail may resemble adult, so cannot mechanically shorten all to wing buds. Candidate research only, no production credit yet.

Historical run records saying guide not read remain true for their earlier stage; this later access adds evidence and triggers targeted review, not retroactive scientific approval. Updated mappings link [65](../knowledge/species/65-tyto-soumagnei.md), [66](../knowledge/species/66-tyto-capensis.md), [67v2](../design/concepts/67-pulsatrix-perspicillata-v2.png), [68](../knowledge/species/68-tyto-tenebricosa.md), planned69 and research-only Ninox strenua. New source documents0; reused document1; six additional chapter mappings. Scientific unresolved conflicts retained.

## #87 Bubo ascalaphus
#87 Bubo ascalaphus — Pharaoh Eagle-Owl
Accessed2026-10-15.
ASC-MIK2013-272: Heimo Mikkola, Owls of the World2013, uploaded book printed272–273/PDF274–275 text actually read. Sandy tawny/rufous upperparts dark mottling, rounded tawny face finely dark-rimmed, yellow to deeporange iris, blackbill, feathered tarsi/toes, shorter pointed tufts than B.bubo; upperbreast dark drop shaftstreaks, fine abdominal markings. Female averages400g heavier (not a height ratio). Whitish down with dull yellow, fluffy mesoptile barred above, slight upperbreast barring/weak tufts. Book monotypic; historical desertorum pale regional variation noted, no new taxon/count inferred. Photo caption juvenile EgyptFebruary notpixelsinspected.
ASC-ALMASAEED2024: Matar Al-Masaeed, Omar Al-Hazza’a, Nader Al-Ghiath, Tareq Qaneer,Nashat Hamidan/RSCN, Breeding Record of Pharaoh Eagle-Owl (Bubo ascalaphus) in the Eastern Desert of Jordan, Jordan Journal of Natural History11(2),2024 pp142–143 (contents141 disagrees witharticleheading). https://www.rscn.org.jo/uploaded_files/journal/679098e3881271737529571.pdf fullPDF downloaded/textread; printed142/PDF72 actually rendered/viewed. Nest at margin gully/runoff EasternRisha,3eggs15March2024; Figure1 shows ground scrape beside shrub/rock not woven nest; earlyApril3chickshatched, flashfloodkilledall. Does NOT establish juvenile appearance or flightability. Primary wild nest record.
ASC-HAJWAL2015-953: Faisal Hajwal, Pharaoh Eagle Owl juvenile with prey, SaudiArabiaApril2015; original photo and caption actually read/viewed https://www.owlpages.com/owls/species.php?i=953 . Fuzzy ochre/tawny juvenile, yelloweyes darkbill, faint bars, weak/no tufts; gripping rock and holding small lizard. Exactage,sex,flightability unknown; one photograph not developmental series. No reproduction of photo inrepo.
Design inference: similarsexplumage, female modestlyfuller notfixedheight ratio; middle fuzzy mesoptilejuvenile onbroadrock gripping naturally, noage/flightclaim, tinybeakopen upwardsolicitation withoutprey copying. Desertwadiusable notcavitytree/wovennest. Preserve uncertainty in exactfeathersequence/toes/sexratio; sciencefalse. Newexternal2, existingbookreuse1 newsectionmapping1. Sourceindex map Buboascalaphus→ASC IDs; historical B.buboascalaphus same species no double count.

### NWFO-MIK2013-254
Heimo Mikkola, Owls of the World: A Photographic Guide (2013), uploaded book printed254–255/PDF256–257, text actually read 2026-10-16. Species Ptilopsis leucotis / historical Otus leucotis (northern taxon). Reference-book synthesis; adult/young description: basal toe half feathered; distal toes dusky-brown; juvenile mesoptile differs from later Togo stage. Mass dimorphism approx30g does not imply scaled height. Juvenile exact age and feather geometry unknown; do not transfer southern P.granti evidence. Supports retaining adult proximal toe feathers and later juvenile formed remiges, not strict scientific pass. Existing book entry/URL applies; no external publicly hosted full book copied. Links [species](../knowledge/species/109-ptilopsis-leucotis.md), [cycle4](../production/runs/2026-10-16T1345+0800-cycle-4.md), target109v3. Reuse NWFO-HEUCLIN-TOGO fresh page/public photo preview; source fact versus design simplification separated.


### CBLO-MIK2013-252
Heimo Mikkola 2013 uploaded Owls of the World guide, printed252–253/PDF254–255; actual TEXT read2026-10-16, photos not extracted. Historical Gymnoglaux lawrencii = Margarobyas lawrencii, Cuba; reference synthesis adult/young. Round untufted head, dark brown iris, pale brows/face, gray-yellow bill; long totally bare yellow-brown tarsi/toes; cream-buff belly black drop-shafts, brown white-tipped upperparts. Mesoptile adultlike less spotted above; down whitish. No sexed size data, juvenile exact age/movement unknown. Supports older partly feathered cavity concept, not precise neonatal reconstruction; no invented dimorphism. Existing uploaded book URL/ID applies. Planned #116; [source-linked run](../production/runs/2026-10-16T1345+0800-cycle-5.md).

### CBLO-AOU-2013A11
Carla Cicero, AOU N&MA Classification Committee proposal2013-A-11 (12Jan2013), PDFpp39–40 actually read2026-10-16 https://americanornithology.org/wp-content/uploads/2020/02/2013-A.pdf . Institutional original taxonomy analysis: Gymnoglaux junior objective synonym of Gymnasio; proposes Margarobyas lawrencii per Olson/Suarez2008. Cuban lawrencii and Puerto Rican nudipes distinct, both lack erectile ear tufts/have bare tarsi. Supports old/current name mapping and avoiding #22 Gymnasio nudipes duplicate. Primary2008 publisher page internal error, not falsely claimed read. Proposal not assumed final modern251-list revision; no scope count change. #116.

### PSC-KOMAR-STUFKENS-2012
- Oliver Komar (2012), “Reflexiones sobre mi primer año observando aves en el campus de la Escuela Agrícola Panamericana (Universidad Zamorano), Valle del Yeguare”, El Esmeralda 1(2), pp59–66; Fig4 p65 photo Paul Stufkens, 27May2012. Institution-hosted original: https://www.aveshonduras.org/wp-content/uploads/2021/11/El-Esmeralda_Vol.-1-No.-2_2012.pdf ; accessed2026-10-16. Primary field observation/photo, Megascops cooperi (old Otus cooperi), Zamorano Honduras, fledgling, exact age/sex unknown. Actual PDF downloaded, relevant text pp59–60,65–66 read, p65 rendered and visually inspected. Figure shows gray soft-headed, finely barred youngster spreading developed barred wings in defense, feet supported on ground; not a flying neonate. Text describes two adults and one fledgling initially in tree; after thrush disturbance youngster fell and had difficulty flying, observer returned it near parents. This is one event, not a universal flight limit or developmental age curve. P60 campus740–815m contradicts treating330m as universal upper elevation. Use ground-supported defensive pose; natural root/leaf setting is design inference, not copied photo location. Sex-size ratio remains unknown. Photo/PDF copyrighted CC BY-NC-ND3.0; no PDF/figure redistributed. Confidence high for this reported event, limited generalization. Related planned #118, [cycle1](../production/runs/2026-10-16T1747+0800-cycle-1.md).

### PSC-OWLPAGES-2020
- Deane Lewis, Pacific Screech Owl—Megascops cooperi, OwlPages, 2020-10-23, https://www.owlpages.com/owls/species.php?s=870 ; accessed2026-10-16. Secondary species account, actual Description/Habitat/Breeding read. Adult pale gray facial disc, yellow irises, greenish bill/cere, prominent barred ear tufts, gray-brown cryptic upperparts; thin shafts and fine vermiculation below. Tarsi feathered to toe bases, fleshy toes with bristles. Supports adult color/feather boundary, not quantified sexual dimorphism or juvenile age. Page elevation330m not accepted as universal ceiling given primary Zamorano record. No newborn morphology or egg schedule extrapolation. Confidence medium; sexed dimensions unknown. Taxon tied to Honduras/CostaRica nominate concept, not Oaxaca lambi; no scope251 change. [Cycle1](../production/runs/2026-10-16T1747+0800-cycle-1.md).

DESO-BENDOV-2017: AmirBenDov,AviamAtar,ErezBaruchi,KochavLevi,NirSapir2017 Breeding biology of Desert Owl in Israel,DutchBirding39:285–307. URLhttps://www.researchgate.net/publication/320517643_Breeding_Biology_of_Desert_Owl_in_Israel . No DOIverified. Access2026-10-18. OriginalauthorpublicHTML highconfidence for field observations, estimatedage notknownhatching; unpublishedexactlocations deliberately not reproduced. Institutionalbibliography https://cris.haifa.ac.il/en/publications/breeding-biology-of-desert-owl-in-israel/ read corroborates authors/year/pages; not independent biologicalevidence.
BCSO-DANTAS-2021: Sidnei M. Dantas, Jason D. Weckstein, John Bates, Joiciane N. Oliveira, Therese A. Catanach & Alexandre Aleixo (2021), "Multi-character taxonomic review, systematics, and biogeography of the Black-capped/Tawny-bellied Screech Owl complex", Zootaxa 4949(3):401–444, published 2021-03-26, DOI https://doi.org/10.11646/zootaxa.4949.3.1. Accessed 2026-10-27. Previously indexed from abstract only; now materially enriched by reading pp.401–402 and the Megascops atricapilla species account p.429. The study used six genes from 49 specimens, 252 specimens and 83 recordings; it maps old names Strix/Otus/Megascops atricapillus to Atlantic-Forest Clade F, gives its verified distribution, records strong plumage variability and a north-south body-size cline. High-confidence primary taxonomy/biogeography. It supplies no juvenile-development sequence or fixed sex-size formula; use it to prevent cross-species trait transfer, not to invent juvenile or individual fine-plumage details.

## 2026-10-18 cycle4 verification update (stable lead IDs retained)
### LZBO-RASMUSSEN-2012-LEAD — now original paper read
Rasmussen, Allen, Collar et al. (2012), Vocal divergence and new species in the Philippine Hawk Owl Ninox philippensis complex, Forktail28:1–20. Original university URL above returned HTTP502 this run; actual public article PDF read at https://www.asianscientist.com/wp-content/uploads/2012/08/Vocal-divergence-and-new-species.pdf (access2026-10-18). Printed p3 methods pools sexes and explicitly does NOT establish sexual size dimorphism; p4 notes immature data sparse, excludes immatures from diagnoses and cannot analyse dichromatism. Printed p12 Fig6a adult photograph, Dolores Quezon Luzon,16January2012, R.J.Quisumbing, actually rendered/viewed. Printed p13 diagnosis/Table7: unmarked brown crown/back; whitish brow; white/buff scapular and large outer-covert spots; longitudinal brown/rufous streaks below, no barring; bright yellow irides/toes, olive-yellow bill, dark claws. Applicability nominate Luzon; old broad N.philippensis includes distinct island species, not transferable. Design: identical adult sex treatment/display scale pending evidence; no invented female enlargement. Figure/photo supports adult visual form; paper is NOT juvenile development or nesting evidence. Related #125 planned, cycle4 record.
### LZBO-EBERSOLE-JUV-LEAD — original page and pixels now read
MarkEbersole (2012), ProjectNoah https://projectnoah.org/spottings/owl-3, actual public HTML and original JPEG read/viewed2026-10-18. Community identification Philippine Hawk-Owl juvenile/Ninox philippensis; observer locality Penablanca,Cagayan,Luzon. Page photographed/published June8,2012; visible camera watermark May3,2012: date conflict retained. Original image linked in page (Google storage), not copied to repository. Visible: yellow irides, olive-yellow bill, buff-brown fluffy crown/breast, dark brown wings with white spots and developed folded flight feathers, yellow scaly toes gripping human finger. Exact age unknown; observer's learning-to-fly is conjecture, not tested flight competence. Community ID/locality compatible with narrow Luzon taxon, not independently genetic/vocal verification. Design inference: feathered juvenile with residual down, short stable natural perch and supported gripping feet; not neonate, not airborne. No nest inference from ground encounter. #125 science remains false. Photo is author field observation, not peer-reviewed age series; retain limitations.

### 2026-10-21 #128 v5 revision audit

- Reused source: **MARS-WESKE-1981** — Weske & Terborgh (1981), original species description, actually re-read pp. 2–3. It supports an irregular whitish hindcrown/nuchal area behind the darker anterior crown, short ear tufts, dark-brown iris and rufous black-rimmed facial disc. It does not give a calibrated display width or a population-wide feather map; the seven-male/one-female specimen series is small.
- Fresh external search used current and historical names (*Megascops marshalli* / *Otus marshalli*) to look for additional institutional photographs and development evidence. No new source with a measured hindcrown-band extent or sex-linked display geometry was actually read, so search-only leads receive no evidentiary credit.
- Design inference, not source fact: the conspicuously broad bright-white v4 band was narrowed, warmed to buff and interrupted by reddish-brown/blackish feather texture. Exact extent remains unknown; image remains scientifically_verified=false.
- Counts: new sources 0; reused sources 1; corrected sources 0. [Cycle record](../production/runs/2026-10-21T0533+0800-cycle-5.md) · [v5](../design/concepts/128-megascops-marshalli-v5.png) · image commit 7617668e91108dd70910a90ed5c1f4b84253d4b0.


### 2026-10-21 #134 v2 revision audit

Kachimareck, WikiAves primary photo record, taken 8 Oct 2014 in Curitiba, Paraná, tagged `Filhote`; original page/full pixels had been actually read and viewed. It supports short gray down/mesoptile, finer diffuse gray-brown throat/chest bars, weak head wisps, a greenish-horn bill and concealment of lower anatomy. Exact age, flight ability and iris colour are not established.
- Fresh current/historical-name web search was performed for juvenile/development evidence. BirdLife and taxonomic/secondary hits added no measured age or feather-sequence facts; no search-only lead is credited as verified evidence.

### EEO-PENTERIANI-2005 reuse — 2026-10-21 cycle 1
Fresh original author PDF was actually read at PDF pp.4–5 / printed pp.159–160, Figures 2–3: 1–3-day chicks are prostrate with first white down, pink bare shoulder/belly patches and pink tarsi/toes; eyes open by 6–7 days; the 10-day photograph retains white down and shows emerging shoulder/scapular/remige/tarsal sheaths. Direct comparison found #03 v5's nestling bill too long and bulky relative to the photographed 10-day stage. #03 v6 therefore changes only the chick bill to a shorter compact gently hooked form; exact bill/body ratio, population variation and sex remain unmeasured. New0/reused1/corrected0; design inference is explicitly separate from the paper facts. [Run](../production/runs/2026-10-21T1134+0800-cycle-1.md) · [v6](../design/concepts/03-bubo-bubo-v6.png). Source: https://www.vincenzopenteriani.org/publications_pdfs/bubo_bubo_ibis1.pdf (copyright link only; no paper copied).


### EEO-PENTERIANI-2005 reuse — 2026-10-21 cycle 2
Fresh targeted web retrieval and original-paper cross-check reused the same stable source, not a duplicate ID. Figure 3 / printed p.160 states and shows the 10-day chick still predominantly dense white down, with localized shoulder/scapular/remige/tarsal sheaths and belly down beginning to cover previously bare skin. #03 v6's large pink shoulder/flank panel read too much like a formed naked wing; #03 v7 reduces that exposed area and restores white-down coverage while retaining a small pink shoulder window and short gray pin-sheaths. The paper gives no numeric skin/down boundary, so the amount reduced is a conservative design inference, not a measurement. Two preliminary texture-only edits failed to remove loop-like rendering and were not archived. New0/reused1/corrected0; persistent loop texture remains a declared visual issue. [Run](../production/runs/2026-10-21T1232+0800-cycle-2.md) · [v7](../design/concepts/03-bubo-bubo-v7.png). DOI 10.1111/j.1474-919x.2004.00381; author PDF https://www.vincenzopenteriani.org/publications_pdfs/bubo_bubo_ibis1.pdf.

### EEO-PENTERIANI-2005 reuse — 2026-10-21 cycle 3
The same original author PDF and Figure 3 / printed p.160 boundary were reused for #03 v8; no duplicate source ID was created. The evidence supports predominantly white down with localized sheaths at the photographed approximately 10-day stage, but does not define a geometric fiber pattern. The v8 change is therefore a rendering correction from repeated closed loops toward irregular matte short down, not a new biological claim. Residual fine arcs, exact feather geometry, age calibration, iris colour, sex and regional variation remain unknown. New0/reused1/corrected0; scientifically_verified=false. [Run](../production/runs/2026-10-21T1326+0800-cycle-3.md) · [v8](../design/concepts/03-bubo-bubo-v8.png). DOI 10.1111/j.1474-919x.2004.00381; author PDF https://www.vincenzopenteriani.org/publications_pdfs/bubo_bubo_ibis1.pdf.

The existing original author PDF Figure 3 / printed p.160 was reused for #03 v9. It supports predominantly white down with localized sheaths at approximately 10 days, not a measured fiber geometry. v9 therefore only reduces residual repeated U/C-shaped rendering artifacts and makes no new biological claim. New0/reused1/corrected0; exact down geometry, age calibration, iris colour, sex and regional variation remain unknown; scientifically_verified=false. The edit therefore removes only residual repeated U/C-shaped and scalloped rendering lines, retaining the compact bill, low supported posture, pink toes, localized shoulder skin and pin-sheaths. ### SPO-MARINGOMEZ-2017 — Spectacled Owl urban breeding observation
Oscar H. Marín-Gómez, Yemay Toro-López, Margarita M. López-García, Javier I. Garzón Zuluaga & Diana M. Santa-Aristizabal (2017), “First records of the Spectacled Owl (*Pulsatrix perspicillata*) in urban areas, with notes on reproduction”, *North-Western Journal of Zoology* 13(2):368–371. Original article PDF: https://biozoojournals.ro/nwjz/content/v13n2/nwjz_e162602_Marin-Gomez.pdf ; accessed 2026-10-21. Actual full article read, especially printed pp.368–370 / PDF pp.1–3 and Fig.2 caption. The authors report two adults with a downy young perched in a tree; the same juvenile was later observed making learning flights, fell, and was returned to a tree. This supports a downy but mobile/perching stage with natural tree support and does not require a hatchling wing-bud depiction. The source does not report exact age, sex, subspecies or measured wing-covert geometry; those remain unknown and the concept is not scientifically verified. Source facts support retaining the formed folded wings and two-foot ledge grip in [#67 v3](../design/concepts/67-pulsatrix-perspicillata-v3.png); the change from round spots to irregular broken bars is a conservative design correction, not a measured reconstruction. [Run](../production/runs/2026-10-21T1930+0800-cycle-2.md).

### 2026-10-21 cycle 2 reuse audit — #67
Reused the already indexed Mikkola/Bouglouan evidence for the white-down, dark-mask juvenile and gray-brown formed wings. New 1 stable source ID (`SPO-MARINGOMEZ-2017`), reused 2, corrected 0. Search-only commercial or social-media hits were not promoted to evidence. Exact age, sex, subspecies and covert geometry remain unknown; `scientifically_verified=false`.


### LZBO-RASMUSSEN-2012-LEAD reuse — 2026-10-21 cycle 3 #144 v2
Rasmussen et al. (2012), original paper “Vocal divergence and new species in the Philippine Hawk Owl *Ninox philippensis* complex”, pp.3–4, printed pp.12–14 and Table 7, was reused after a fresh current-name/old-name web search. The paper supports a spotted adult crown and variable brown-and-white ventral streaking in *N. spilocephala*; adult diagnoses exclude immatures, and pooled sexes do not establish reversed dimorphism. Applied only to replace #144 adults' coarse repeated teardrop marks with finer irregular broken longitudinal streaking. The older immature remains occluded because exact age, ventral pattern, wing/foot/tail development and action ability are not documented. New 0; reused 1; corrected 0; `scientifically_verified=false`. [Run](../production/runs/2026-10-21T2334+0800-cycle-3.md) · [v2](../design/concepts/144-ninox-spilocephala-v2.png).


### 2026-10-22 cycle 4 reuse audit — #113 v5
Fresh targeted search and actual page reading reused two already-known species accounts without creating duplicate IDs: the Australian Government Christmas Island National Park page (adult bright-yellow eyes, tawny-orange upperparts, orange-and-white barred underparts; institutional field photograph) and OwlPages (adult whitish breast finely barred rufous, dark tail with rufous bands). Neither source measures an older juvenile's exact feather map, age or flight ability. Direct v4 inspection showed the central older juvenile's chest/belly as highly regular stacked adult-like scallop bands. v5 therefore makes a bounded rendering correction only: softer paler downy body texture with fine irregular broken rufous bars, while retaining formed wing/tail and natural two-foot branch support. Exact age, sex, flight ability and juvenile pattern remain unknown; `scientifically_verified=false`. New 0; reused 2; corrected 0. ### ISOW-NEST-LUCKNOW-2019 — Indian Scops Owl nesting ecology (limited abstract record)
- Stable ID: `ISOW-NEST-LUCKNOW-2019`
- Title: “Nesting Ecology of Indian Scops Owl (*Otus bakkamoena*) in Lucknow”
- Author/year: metadata on accessible record incomplete; 2019 publication record; accessed 2026-10-22.
- URL: https://www.researchgate.net/publication/338448693_NESTING_ECOLOGY_OF_INDIAN_SCOPS_OWL_OTUS_BAKKAMOENA_IN_LUCKNOW
- Type/read extent: original-study record and abstract-level text actually read; full paper/pages were not accessible in this cycle.
- Taxon/stage/topic: *Otus bakkamoena*; breeding/nest site; no juvenile morphology.
- Fact: 21 nests were reported; most were in tree cavities, notably old neem, khair and cluster fig trees.
- Limitation/confidence: abstract-level evidence only; does not provide a read cavity diagram, height, dimensions, floor-material quantity or nestling stage. Moderate confidence for natural-cavity direction, no credit for fine geometry.
- Design link: supports replacing #84 v2's conspicuous board-built box with a natural tree hollow in [#84 v5](../design/concepts/84-otus-bakkamoena-v5.png). The exact hollow shown is conservative design inference. [Cycle record](../production/runs/2026-10-22T0048+0800-cycle-5.md).

### 2026-10-22 cycle 5 reuse audit — #84 v5
OwlPages photo record of an Indian Scops Owl at a tree-hollow entrance was actually read/viewed at page-caption level and reused. Combined with `ISOW-NEST-LUCKNOW-2019`, it supports a natural-cavity setting, not a measured cavity blueprint. New 1 limited source; reused 1; corrected 0. Exact tree species, dimensions, height, floor material and juvenile stage remain unknown; `scientifically_verified=false`. Image commit 218f2ee8ed77007bfff1408d9c63daea163156b1.


### NINOX-NATALIS-ADULT-BARRING-2026 — Christmas Island Boobook adult plumage cross-check
- Stable ID: NINOX-NATALIS-ADULT-BARRING-2026
- Sources actually read: Australian Government Christmas Island National Park bird profile (accessed 2026-10-22), https://www.dcceew.gov.au/parks-heritage/christmas-island/nature/birds ; OwlPages species account (accessed 2026-10-22), https://www.owlpages.com/owls/species.php?s=620 ; Australian Government threat-abatement plan (PDF species listing only; accessed 2026-10-22), https://www.dcceew.gov.au/sites/default/files/documents/exotic-rodents.pdf

- Fact: institutional account supports bright yellow eyes, tawny-orange upperparts and orange/white barred underparts. OwlPages describes the breast as whitish and finely barred rufous.
- Limitation/confidence: descriptions do not prescribe exact bar count, spacing or individual geometry; no sex-specific ventral difference was established. High confidence for fine rufous-on-pale barring direction, low confidence for exact geometry.
- Design inference/application: in [#113 v6](../design/concepts/113-ninox-natalis-v6.png), only adult ventral markings were changed from broad mechanical bands to finer irregular broken bars. All other features and the v5 older juvenile were retained. scientifically_verified=false.

### NINOX-PHILIPPENSIS-TAXON-DESIGN-2026 — Luzon Boobook taxonomy and concept boundaries
- Stable ID: NINOX-PHILIPPENSIS-TAXON-DESIGN-2026
- Rasmussen et al. (2012), “Vocal divergence and new species in the Philippine Hawk Owl Ninox philippensis complex,” Forktail; original taxonomic paper, abstract and diagnostic summary read; https://news.msu.edu/media/documents/2012/08/0cd74c7a-3869-429d-92e2-f4ff62cd82e8.pdf
- U.S. Fish & Wildlife Service, Luzon Boobook species profile; institutional taxonomy record read; https://www.fws.gov/species/luzon-boobook-ninox-philippensis
- eBird, Luzon Boobook species account; current range/habitat and adult identification text read; https://ebird.org/species/phihao1
- BirdLife Data Zone factsheet; current species and endemic resident-range treatment read; https://datazone.birdlife.org/species/factsheet/luzon-boobook-ninox-philippensis
- Taxon mapping: current Ninox philippensis is separated from the former broad Philippine Hawk-Owl complex; already archived Ninox spilocephala remains a distinct current species. Older broad N. philippensis usage is a historical crosswalk, not merged here.
- Supported adult direction: small compact boobook, no ear tufts, yellow eyes, brown upperparts with restrained pale spotting, pale underparts with broad brown streaking, white eyebrow/moustachial facial marks; sexes visually similar.
- Limitation: no strong primary juvenile-plumage sequence was found. The older juvenile in [#145 v1](../design/concepts/145-ninox-philippensis-v1.png) is conservative design inference, not an age reconstruction. Exact sex-size ratio and subspecies pattern remain unknown; scientifically_verified=false.
- Cycle audit: new sources 4; reused 0; corrected 0. Image commit 5514fc0f750c7fa6a34444f8bbea128e62500fc5. [Run](../production/runs/2026-10-22T1126+0800-cycle-1.md).

设计决策：#150 v1采用近等尺度成年雌雄；以暗红褐上体、肩羽横斑、无显著白眉、浅素喉与横斑下体区分上一组新不列颠种。初稿幼鸟具有过长成人式层叠飞羽，已编辑为短圆紧凑翼、柔化体纹及双足自然支撑后复查。未取得物种专属幼鸟序列、性别比例或精确巢位证据，故 `scientifically_verified=false`。关联：[物种记录](../knowledge/species/150-ninox-variegata.md)、[v1](../design/concepts/150-ninox-variegata-v1.png)、[运行记录](../production/runs/2026-10-22T1728+0800-cycle-6.md)。


## #151 *Athene jacquinoti* (historical *Ninox jacquinoti*) — West Solomons Owl — 2026-10-22
| 稳定ID | 来源 | 年份 | 类型 | 物种/阶段 | 实际读取与位置 | 关键事实 | 局限与用途 |
|---|---|---:|---|---|---|---|---|
| WSO-EBIRD-ID-2026 | Cornell Lab/eBird, [West Solomons Owl](https://ebird.org/species/solboo1) | 页面现行版，访问2026-10-22 | 机构识别页及照片索引 | *Athene jacquinoti*，成年/分布 | Identification，第56–63行及照片索引 | 小型橙眼鸮，褐色上体有浅色小斑、下体较浅；偏好发育良好的森林，以低地为主，最高约1500米；分布自Bougainville、Buka、Choiseul至Florida Island；已从旧广义“Solomons Boobook”拆分。 | 不提供性别测量或幼鸟序列；支持当前名称、成年识别、橙眼和森林背景。可信度：高。 |
| WSO-OWLPAGES-2020 | Deane Lewis / OwlPages, [Solomons Boobook](https://www.owlpages.com/owls/species.php?s=3320) | 更新2020-11-07，访问2026-10-22 | 专题物种页，二级来源 | *Ninox jacquinoti*，成年/旧名 | Introduction、Information，第49–80行 | 小至中型、无耳簇；旧名包括West Solomons Hawk Owl/Boobook；记录所罗门群岛分布并链接Mikkola 2013。 | 形态信息有限，非原始研究；只用于无耳簇和旧名交叉核验，不支持幼鸟或性别方向。可信度：中。 |
| WSO-WBN-TAX-2026 | World Bird Names, [Solomons Boobook / *Ninox jacquinoti*](https://www.worldbirdnames.com/bird/solomons-boobook/9461.html) | 页面现行版，访问2026-10-22 | 分类历史聚合 | *N. jacquinoti*，分类/旧名 | Taxonomy与type locality，第49–77行 | 列出 *Ninox jacquinoti*、原组合 *Athene jacquinoti*、1850年描述和所罗门群岛模式产地；保留旧广义亚种历史线索。 | 与eBird当前属级处理不同，页面还保留旧广义分类；仅用于名称映射，不能用作形态或幼鸟证据。可信度：中。 |

设计决策：#151 v1使用eBird当前名称 *Athene jacquinoti*，同时在档案保留项目图鉴常见旧名 *Ninox jacquinoti*。成年以橙眼、无耳簇、褐色浅斑上体、浅下体和森林背景为证据边界；更细的眉、喉和胸腹纹理仅作照片导向的保守设计，不视为定量诊断。首稿幼鸟出现过长层叠飞羽，编辑为更短圆、柔软的发育翼，翼端止于足部上方，并复查双足支撑。没有物种专属幼鸟序列、稳定性别二型或巢位证据，故 `scientifically_verified=false`。关联：[物种记录](../knowledge/species/151-athene-jacquinoti.md)、[v1](../design/concepts/151-athene-jacquinoti-v1.png)、[运行记录](../production/runs/2026-10-22T1828+0800-cycle-1.md)。


## 2026-10-22 · #153 Maned Owl — *Jubula lettii*

### MANED-ML-531449801-2023
- Title/institution: [Macaulay Library asset ML531449801 — Maned Owl](https://macaulaylibrary.org/asset/531449801); Cornell Lab of Ornithology
- Date/access: observation 2023-02-04; accessed 2026-10-22
- Type: institution-hosted field photograph record with observer notes
- Applies to: *Jubula lettii*, adult/unknown birds and one mobile immature; Equatorial Guinea primary forest
- Evidence actually read: one immature flew a short distance and perched; it retained white juvenile facial feathers. Notes record sandy/rufous back and wings, large ear tufts, longish tail with underside bars, orange eyes, yellowish feet, reddish upper-breast wash and fine black vertical flank streaks.
- Limits/confidence: page explicitly leaves age and sex unspecified; one observation cannot define a full developmental sequence or sexual dimorphism. High for this dated individual and locality, medium for generalisation.
- Design support: older branch-supported immature with white juvenile facial feathering; adults use the recorded diagnostic palette and silhouette.

### MANED-ITIS-555440-2026
- Title/institution: [ITIS report TSN 555440 — *Jubula lettii*](https://www.itis.gov/servlet/SingleRpt/SingleRpt?print_version=PRT&search_topic=TSN&search_value=555440&source=to_print)
- Date/access: current report; accessed 2026-10-22
- Type: government taxonomic database
- Applies to: species identity and historical name
- Evidence actually read: *Jubula lettii* is valid; synonym *Bubo letti*.
- Limits/confidence: taxonomy only; does not support morphology, sex or juvenile development. High for naming use.
- Design support: current label *Jubula lettii* and legacy-name research mapping.

### MANED-BHL-HABITAT-1992
- Title/source: [On the vocal behaviour and habitat of the maned owl *Jubula lettii* in south-western Congo](https://www.biodiversitylibrary.org/part/77066), *Bulletin of the British Ornithologists’ Club* 112:213–218
- Date/access: 1992; accessed 2026-10-22
- Type: original field article, BHL-hosted metadata/article access
- Applies to: habitat and vocal-behaviour research context
- Evidence actually read: article identity, publication details, pages and habitat focus.
- Limits/confidence: this run did not extract a juvenile description from article pages; therefore it is not used for juvenile plumage. High for bibliographic/habitat scope.
- Design support: Central African forest habitat context only.

### MANED-BIRDLIFE-LIST-2026
- Title/institution: [BirdLife International list of owl species](https://www.birdlife.org/birds/list-of-owl-species/)
- Date/access: current page; accessed 2026-10-22
- Type: conservation/taxonomic list
- Applies to: *Jubula lettii*
- Evidence actually read: Maned Owl *Jubula lettii* is listed as Data Deficient.
- Limits/confidence: list entry supplies no juvenile sequence or sex measurements. High for the displayed category only.
- Design support: requires explicit uncertainty labels and forbids claiming scientific approval from a concept image.


## 2026-10-22 · #154 Jamaican Owl — *Pseudoscops grammicus*

### JAM-PEREGRINE-2026
- Title/institution: [Jamaican Owl](https://peregrinefund.org/explore-raptors-species/owls/jamaican-owl), The Peregrine Fund
- Date/access: current page; accessed 2026-10-22
- Type: raptor-research institution species account
- Applies to: adult, habitat, nest and evidence gaps
- Evidence actually read: Jamaica endemic, chiefly lowland open woodland/forest edge; dark eyes, bright rufous body, fine black breast streaks, white/black-edged facial disk and prominent ear tufts; tree cavities or forks and up to two eggs.
- Limits/confidence: page explicitly says nestling growth, first flight age and parental care remain unknown; no sex measurements. High for account facts, deliberately insufficient for exact juvenile age.
- Design support: adult recognition and wooded-edge/cavity setting; no invented sex dimorphism or fledging age.

### JAM-BIRDSEYE-YOUNG-2018
- Title/source: [BirdsEye Photography Jamaican Owl review index](https://birdseye.photo/photos/review/3591/jamaican_owl/)
- Date/access: site copyright 2018; accessed 2026-10-22
- Type: species-labelled image index
- Applies to: adult and downy young
- Evidence actually read: two adult entries and one *Pseudoscops grammicus* entry labelled “Downy young”.
- Limits/confidence: visible index does not provide day-age, measurements or continuous feather sequence. Medium; supports stage existence only.
- Design support: conservative downy, age-unknown young bird with no mature remiges.

### JAM-BIRDLIFE-2026
- Title/institution: [Jamaican Owl species factsheet](https://datazone.birdlife.org/species/factsheet/jamaican-owl-pseudoscops-grammicus), BirdLife International
- Date/access: current page; accessed 2026-10-22
- Type: conservation/taxonomic factsheet
- Applies to: species identity/factsheet mapping
- Evidence actually read: page identity and assessment framework; morphology fields were not exposed in the readable dynamic page.
- Limits/confidence: not used for plumage or juvenile design. High for page identity only.
- Design support: stable factsheet linkage, no extra morphological inference.

Confidence: medium. |
| SLBO-KW-SYNTH-2008 | König & Weick account as quoted in current species synthesis, [Sri Lanka bay owl](https://de.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceylonmaskeneule) | 2008 source attribution; page accessed 2026-10-23; tertiary gateway to specialist monograph | adult comparison and nesting | About 29 cm; darker and browner than *P. badius*, densely white/black spotted above, conspicuously barred wings/tail, pink-washed heart-shaped face; tree cavity and 2–3 white eggs reported. | Full monograph pages were not directly accessible in this run; marked as secondary/tertiary evidence and not used for exact juvenile anatomy. Confidence: medium. |

Design boundary: #159 v1 uses near-equal adult scale, a pink-washed heart face, very dark eyes, dark brown densely spotted upperparts and clearly barred wings/tail to distinguish it from #158. The juvenile is an age-unknown, naturally supported cavity-rim chick with soft down, short rounded developing wings and a very short tail; no exact age, flight ability or continuous feather sequence is asserted. No stable visible sexual dimorphism or quantitative sex ratio was found. scientifically_verified=false.


## 2026-10-23 · #84 Indian Scops Owl — *Otus bakkamoena* v6 facial-disc revision

| Stable ID | Source | Date/type | Scope | Evidence actually read | Limits / design use |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ISO-EBIRD-ID-2026 | Cornell Lab/eBird, [Indian Scops-Owl](https://ebird.org/species/insowl1) | current identification page; accessed 2026-10-23 | adult identification/photos | Medium-sized scops owl with prominent ear tufts, dark eyes, variable grey-to-brown plumage and a thin black border around the facial disc. | Does not define pixel-level feather geometry or juvenile sequence. High confidence for dark eyes and thin disc boundary. |
| ISO-PF-FACE-2026 | The Peregrine Fund, [Indian Scops-owl](https://peregrinefund.org/explore-raptors-species/owls/indian-scops-owl) | current raptor institution account; accessed 2026-10-23 | adult facial disc/morphs | Grey-brown and rufous morphs; small ear tufts and darker feathers around the facial disc. | General account, no exact periocular feather measurements. High confidence for qualitative disc treatment. |
| ISO-OWLPAGES-DESC-2020 | Deane Lewis / OwlPages, [Indian Scops Owl](https://www.owlpages.com/owls/species.php?s=760) | updated 2020; accessed 2026-10-23 | adult description | Facial disc pale grey-brown with a distinct blackish rim; forehead and eyebrows paler; eyes hazel-brown to dark brown; long prominent ear tufts with dark outer margins. | Secondary specialist synthesis, not a calibrated colour chart. Supports removing white circular eye “goggles” while preserving the broader pale disc and dark rim. Medium-high confidence. |

Revision decision: v5 actual review showed a large pale circular feather ring around each adult eye, visually reading as goggles rather than one broader facial disc. v6 targets only those thick circular periocular rings: blend them into a continuous pale grey-buff disc, preserve dark eyes and a thin black outer disc rim, and retain the natural tree cavity, juvenile, feet, labels and all other composition. This is a visual correction, not scientific approval.

### MINBOO-EBIRD-ID-2026 — Mindanao Boobook identification
Cornell Lab of Ornithology / eBird, “Mindanao Boobook (*Ninox spilocephala*)”, current species account; https://ebird.org/species/minboo1 ; accessed 2026-10-23. Institutional identification account. Actual page text read: small lowland-forest owl on Mindanao; dark-brown head/upperparts with wing speckles, reddish-brown chest, white belly with thick reddish-brown streaking, yellowish eyes; smaller than Chocolate Boobook and lacks white on the chest. Applies to adults and habitat only. The account does not document juvenile age, posture, sex measurements or foot support, so those remain unknown. Supports preserving the already-reviewed v2 plumage and forest setting; it does not justify changing the immature plumage.

### MINBOO-AVIBASE-TAXON-2026 — current/old-name mapping
Avibase / Bird Studies Canada, “Mindanao Boobook *Ninox spilocephala*”, https://avibase.bsc-eoc.org/species.jsp?avibaseid=581CA4FF6396A3BC ; accessed 2026-10-23. Taxonomic database and original-description index. Actual page read: current combination *Ninox spilocephala* Tweeddale 1879, former English usage Mindanao Hawk-Owl, range Mindanao/Basilan/Dinagat/Siargao, original-description link and type locality Zamboanga, Mindanao. No juvenile-development or sex-size evidence. Supports current-name/old-name mapping only.

### 2026-10-23 cycle 5 evidence boundary — #144 v3
Fresh research added `MINBOO-EBIRD-ID-2026` and `MINBOO-AVIBASE-TAXON-2026`; reused `LZBO-RASMUSSEN-2012-LEAD` for the split-species diagnosis and older-immature boundary. The v2 plumage remains invariant. The only design edit is to reveal a short natural branch under the older immature and both feet gripping it, resolving ambiguous support without asserting new juvenile anatomy. Sources new/reused/corrected: 2/1/0. Exact juvenile age, soft-part colors, wing/tail sequence and quantified sex ratio remain unknown; scientifically_verified=false.


## 2026-10-23 · #109 Northern White-faced Owl — *Ptilopsis leucotis* v6
### NWFO-AVIBASE-TAX-2026
Avibase / Birds Canada, “Northern White-faced Owl *Ptilopsis leucotis*”, https://avibase.bsc-eoc.org/species.jsp?avibaseid=E561A180ED8942F7 ; accessed 2026-10-23. Taxonomic database. Actual page read: current taxonomic concept consistently uses *Ptilopsis leucotis* and retains historical *Otus leucotis*. It provides no juvenile age, feather sequence or sex measurements; high confidence for naming only.

Cycle boundary: reused `NWFO-OWLPAGES-2020` for adult white black-rimmed facial disc, long dark-tipped tufts, amber/orange eyes and horn-yellow bill. No directly read continuous species-specific juvenile series was found. v6 targets only repeated bright scalloped juvenile wing edges; exact age, continuous feather geometry, sex ratio and toe-feather extent remain unknown. Sources new/reused/corrected 1/1/0; scientifically_verified=false.


## 2026-10-23 · #117 Sulawesi Scops Owl — *Otus manadensis* v6

### SLSO-EBIRD-JUV-2026
- Title: Sulawesi Scops-Owl *Otus manadensis*
- Author/institution: Cornell Lab of Ornithology / eBird
- Year: n.d.; accessed 2026-10-23
- URL: https://ebird.org/species/susowl1
- Type: institutional species account; secondary identification summary
- Species/stage: *Otus manadensis*; juvenile
- Actual content read: juvenile is described as heavily barred on back and crown; adult account also supports small brown owl, yellow eyes and feathered ear tufts.
- Location: Identification text on species account; no stable page number.
- Limits/confidence: useful for broad juvenile pattern and soft parts, but not a primary developmental study and gives no exact age, sex ratio or continuous feather sequence. Moderate confidence for design direction only.
- Design decision: retain heavier crown/mantle barring while editing only regular decorative ventral/covert contours.

### SLSO-OISEAUX-JUV-2026
- Title: Sulawesi Scops-owl — *Otus manadensis*
- Author/institution: Oiseaux-Birds / Françoise V.
- Year: n.d.; accessed 2026-10-23
- URL: https://www.oiseaux-birds.com/card-sulawesi-scops-owl.html
- Type: secondary species account
- Species/stage: *Otus manadensis*; juvenile and adult morphology
- Actual content read: juvenile resembles adults but has heavier barring on crown and mantle; adult account provides 19–23 cm and 83–93 g ranges and yellow eyes.
- Location: Description and juvenile paragraph; no stable page number.
- Limits/confidence: compiled account, not primary ontogeny evidence; exact age, sex ratio and feather sequence absent. Moderate confidence for broad pattern only.
- Design decision: corroborates preservation of crown/mantle barring; does not justify invented age-specific wing structure.

Cycle boundary: new/reused/corrected source IDs 2/2/0. Reused `SLSO-MANAHAMPI-2012` and `SLSO-MIK2013-194`. v6 is a juvenile-only correction of repeated ventral/covert arcs. Exact age, sex ratio, continuous wing/tail sequence and flight ability remain unknown; `scientifically_verified=false`.
