## 2026-10-29 — #86 *Otus elegans* v4 — 30-day body/wing growth decoupling and trait-specific RSD

- **ELE-SAWADA2021-ARDEA (new primary full-study anchor):** Akira Sawada, Kana Akatani & Masaoki Takagi, “Growth Curves of Ryukyu Scops Owl Nestlings, an Owl Species with Asynchronous Hatching and Reversed Sexual Dimorphism,” *Ardea* 109:229–241 (2021), DOI [10.5253/arde.v109i3.a2](https://doi.org/10.5253/arde.v109i3.a2). The corresponding full chapter was actually read in Sawada’s Hokkaido University dissertation, Chapter 3, printed pp.42–91 / PDF pp.46–95, especially printed pp.44–49 and growth tables/figures on printed pp.54–74; accessed 2026-10-29. Ninety-nine Minami-daito nestlings were measured for 32 days. The study states a roughly one-month nestling period; body mass and tarsus approach their asymptotes near fledging while wing length remains on a different, later growth trajectory. Sex, hatching order, brood and year affect curves, but many differences are small. High for growth boundary and population, but it does not provide a continuous calibrated plumage-photo series or individual first-flight threshold.
- **ELE-SAWADA2021-RSD-FULL (corrected access/status, primary):** Akira Sawada et al., “Reversed sexual size dimorphism in the Ryukyu Scops Owl *Otus elegans* on Minami-daito Island,” *Ornithological Science* 20:15–26 (2021), DOI [10.2326/osj.20.15](https://doi.org/10.2326/osj.20.15), official full PDF https://www.jstage.jst.go.jp/article/osj/20/1/20_15/_pdf ; printed pp.15–26 actually read 2026-10-29. Table 1 on printed p.17 uses 770 individuals/17 years: breeding-season mean female mass 96.18 g vs male 85.93 g, while natural wing length is nearly equal (155.74 vs 155.65 mm); females average longer/heavier in some traits, males slightly longer in tarsus/head, and several dimorphism directions vary by year. High for trait-specific adult scale; it directly rejects a single “female larger in every dimension” drawing rule.
- **ELE-SAWADA2024-RAIN (new primary limitation source):** Akira Sawada et al., “Do weather conditions during the brood rearing period affect the survival of owl chicks on a subtropical island?”, *Ornithological Science* 23(2):65–76 (2024), DOI [10.2326/osj.23.65](https://doi.org/10.2326/osj.23.65), official J-STAGE article/PDF; abstract and article record actually read 2026-10-29. Six years/806 chicks: higher precipitation was associated with lower chick mass and shorter tarsi/wings, and heavy rain increased mortality. High for developmental plasticity/uncertainty, not a plumage plate or an instruction to depict an unhealthy chick.
- **Reused:** `ELE-AKATANI2011-BR` (printed pp.4–5; bare grey toes, yellow iris, deep-grey bill, same adult plumage, Casuarina cavities/shelterbelt and cane-field context); `ELE-AKATANI2011-JRR` (printed pp.315–323; cavity and habitat detail); `ELE-MIK2013-160` (printed pp.160–161/PDF pp.162–163; historical photographic adult/mesoptile baseline).
- **Correction/application:** v3 correctly separated near-adult body growth from immature wing growth but treated “short wing” qualitatively. V4 anchors the selected stage explicitly at the approximately 30-day late-nestling/nest-departure boundary and keeps body/tarsi near adult while wings remain clearly incomplete. Adults remain near-equal in displayed height/wing length; the female may be subtly deeper-bodied for average mass, never uniformly enlarged. Because no calibrated daily plumage series or first-flight measure was found, the juvenile retains soft developing mesoptile, shorter rounded folded wings and full cavity-lip support without a flight claim.
- **Unknown/limits:** exact individual day, continuous feather-tract sequence, tail growth, iris development, first-flight competence, sex of the illustrated young and transfer beyond Minami-daito remain unknown. Concept remains scientifically unverified.
- Related: [species card](../knowledge/species/86-otus-elegans.md) · [v3](../design/concepts/86-otus-elegans-v3.png) · [run](../production/runs/20261029T1325+0800-cycle-2.md).


## 2026-10-29 — #91 *Tyto javanica delicatula* v3 — late pre-fledgling wing and locomotion correction

### EBO-MCLAUGHLIN-DEBUS-2025 — full-text verification/enrichment (existing stable source ID)

- **Title:** Breeding biology of the Eastern Barn Owl *Tyto javanica* in the western Riverina, New South Wales
- **Authors:** McLaughlin & Debus
- **Year / source type:** 2025; peer-reviewed primary field study, *Australian Field Ornithology* 42:116–124
- **DOI / original URL:** https://doi.org/10.20938/afo42116124 · https://afo.birdlife.org.au/afo/index.php/afo/article/download/2371/2402
- **Accessed and actually read:** 2026-10-29; printed pp.116, 118–121, 123 (PDF pages 1, 3–6, 8)
- **Taxon / stage:** Australian *Tyto javanica*; 14 nests; nestlings from hollow-climbing through first week post-fledging
- **Source facts:** p.116 treats the Australian taxon as *T. javanica* and cautions against uncritical transfer from other regional forms. Thirteen of 14 nests were tree hollows and one a hay shed (p.118). Young ascended inside hollows and exercised wings at the entrance at about 31–45 days; one used bill, claws and wings to climb back (pp.120–121). Earliest young outside a hollow was 46 days, oldest still inside 52 days, estimated range 46–57 days and mean about 53 days (p.119). From about 46 days young climbed, jumped, flapped or flew around the nest tree/ground, and recently fledged birds could fly reasonably well though reluctant to flush (p.121). The authors flag uncertainty in the estimated mean (p.123).
- **Design decision:** Correct v2's short/rounded juvenile wings. V3 uses a conservative late pre-fledgling (~46–52 d) with developed fresh flight wings, residual down, near-adult scale and a two-foot supported hollow-climb/step. It does not claim an exact individual day, sex, tract sequence or universal fledging mean.
- **Limitations / confidence:** High for the study population and behavioural range; exact pictured individual timing and plumage sequence remain unknown. Design inference is explicitly separate from source facts.
- **Links:** [#91 species card](../knowledge/species/91-tyto-javanica-delicatula.md) · v3 run `../production/runs/20261029T1536+0800-cycle-1.md`

### EBO-AUSTRALIAN-MUSEUM-2026 — reused institutional morphology/habitat account

- **Institution / URL:** Australian Museum · https://australian.museum/learn/animals/birds/eastern-barn-owl/
- **Accessed:** 2026-10-29; Identification and Habitat sections
- **Facts used:** *Tyto javanica*; white heart-shaped disc; sandy-orange/light-grey upperparts; white/cream underparts; black spotting; females slightly larger; open often arid farm/heath/light woodland habitat.
- **Boundary:** “Young similar to adults” is not an age sequence and was not used to invent natal-down timing.

### EBO-MUSEUMSVICTORIA-B35989-2023 — new current-taxonomy specimen record

- **Title / institution:** Specimen B 35989 *Tyto javanica delicatula* (Gould, 1837) · Museums Victoria Collections
- **URL:** https://collections.museumsvictoria.com.au/specimens/2621414
- **Year / accessed:** specimen prepared 2023; accessed 2026-10-29
- **Source type / taxon:** museum specimen catalogue; female skin; *Tyto javanica delicatula*, preferred common name Eastern Barn Owl
- **Fact supported:** Current subspecies mapping and common name for Australian material; supports the old-name crosswalk from *Tyto alba delicatula* retained in the card.
- **Limitation:** Single adult dry skin; not juvenile-development evidence and not used for sex-size magnitude.

**Source accounting:** new 1 / reused 2 / corrected 1. The uploaded Mikkola guide was not newly accessible in this cycle; its prior card mapping is retained but does not replace the exact-taxon juvenile evidence above.


## 2026-10-29 — #92 *Ninox japonica totogo* v3 — documented 25–28-day fledging and juvenile-tail boundary

### JAP-LIN2012 — full-text reuse, page-specific juvenile-stage boundary

- **Title:** Breeding ecology of the Northern Boobook *Ninox japonica totogo* in central Taiwan
- **Authors / source:** Wen-Loung Lin, Si-Min Lin & Hui-Yun Tseng; *Forktail* 28:164–166 (2012)
- **Original URL:** https://web.ntnu.edu.tw/~treehopper/lib/publications_lib_pdf/nonox_et_al_2012.pdf
- **Accessed/read:** 2026-10-29; all three pages, especially printed pp.164–165 / PDF pp.1–2
- **Facts:** The paper maps the Far East form to *N. japonica* and the southern resident to *N. j. totogo* (p.164). Nine successful nests had a mean nestling period 25.9 ± 1.1 days, range 25–28 days; post-fledging observations continued every 3–7 days (p.165). Ten measured pairs showed no significant adult male–female difference; 20-day nestlings averaged 143.6 ± 11.6 g (p.165). Nest sites were tree cavities in Taiwan hardwood/secondary-forest mosaics.
- **Design use:** V3 selects a conservative ~25–28-day just-fledged bird, formed wings/tail and supported step from a cavity; adults overlap in scale. The study does not provide a continuous plumage plate, so feather colours are cross-checked separately.
- **Limitations:** Population/study-specific; no exact individual feather sequence or fledgling motor score.

### JAP-FUKAI2016 — new primary age-determination paper

- **Title:** Age determination of Brown Hawk-Owl (*Ninox scutulata*)
- **Author / year / journal:** Norio Fukai, 2016, *Bulletin of the Japanese Bird Banding Association* 28:22–28
- **DOI / PDF:** https://doi.org/10.14491/jbba.ms073 · https://www.jstage.jst.go.jp/article/jbba/28/1/28_MS073/_pdf
- **Accessed/read:** 2026-10-29; PDF pp.22–28, especially pp.22–25 / PDF pp.1–4, Table 1 and Fig.1
- **Taxon/stage:** Historic *N. s. japonica* and *N. s. totogo* (= current *N. japonica* complex); juvenile, first-winter and adult tails
- **Facts:** Juvenile rectrices taper and have a broad pale terminal fringe approximately as wide as the inner pale bands, whereas adults have broader rounded rectrices and a narrow whiter terminal fringe (pp.22, 24–25; Fig.1). Table 1 includes three juvenile *totogo* and the discussion reports juvenile-type tails in all analysed juvenile *japonica* and *totogo* images; juvenile tail feathers persist into first winter.
- **Design use:** V3 makes the juvenile tail visible and uses the tapered/broad-fringe diagnostic. It does not transfer adult moult timing into a precise nestling-day feather schedule.
- **Limitations:** Tail diagnostic, not a full growth series; exceptions such as accidental replacement are noted in the paper.

### JAP-TAIEOL2013 — reused Taiwan institutional account

- **Institution / URL:** Taiwan Encyclopedia of Life · https://taieol.tw/pages/244954/articles
- **Facts used:** Yellow iris, no ear tufts, dark-brown upperparts, 3–5 white shoulder patches, white underparts with broad dark longitudinal streaks, pale-cream tarsal feathers, yellow toes with sparse bristles; immature underparts pale yellow-brown; old-name mapping *N. scutulata totogo/japonica*.
- **Limitations:** Morphology/taxonomy synthesis, not a day-by-day nestling series.

### JAP-NTM-TMAV2568 — new museum collection account

- **Title / institution:** Brown Hawk Owl, collection TMAV2568 · National Taiwan Museum / Ministry of Culture
- **URL:** https://collections.culture.tw/ntm_collectionsweb/EN/Collection?GID=06M6MZ06MB
- **Accessed:** 2026-10-29; taxonomy, description and habitat fields
- **Facts used:** Current catalogue *Ninox japonica totogo*; adults dark brown with small interocular and shoulder white patches, yellow iris, grey-black bill, coarse ventral streaks; juvenile paler with fewer/blurry streaks; Taiwan lowland forest/plantation context.
- **Limitations:** Adult specimen plus institutional synthesis; not evidence for precise fledgling age or sex ratio.

**Source accounting:** new 2 / reused 2 / corrected 1. Correction: v1–v2 hid or left age-vague the juvenile tail; v3 exposes the juvenile-type tapered/broad-fringe rectrices using direct age-determination evidence. Existing Mikkola pp.466–467 is retained as a base morphology cross-check, not used alone to invent fledgling behaviour.


## 2026-10-29 — #93 *Aegolius harrisii iheringi* v2 — documented 24-day pre-fledging stage

### HAR-BODRATI2019 — reused exact-taxon primary nest study, stage correction

- **Title:** Nidificación de la Lechucita Canela (*Aegolius harrisii*) en Misiones, Argentina
- **Authors / journal:** Alejandro Bodrati, Milka R. Gómez, Carlos A. Ferreyra & Kristina L. Cockle; *Ornitología Neotropical* 30:151–156 (2019)
- **DOI / original URL:** https://doi.org/10.58843/ornneo.v30i0.479 · https://ornneo.ornitologianeotropical.org/index.php/ornneo/article/view/479
- **Accessed:** full paper and Fig.1 already read/rendered for v1; actively rechecked 2026-10-29 through the journal/author-indexed record and result text
- **Taxon/stage:** monitored *A. harrisii iheringi* nest, Misiones; hatch through fledging
- **Facts:** old woodpecker cavity 9.6 m high in dead grapia in Atlantic Forest; entrance about 8×8 cm. Hatchlings had very sparse dark-grey natal down; facial disc began at ~10 days; developing feathers at 10–14 days; by 24 days young resembled adults; all three fledged at 35–37 days.
- **Design correction:** V2 uses ~24 days—mostly feathered and disc-bearing but still cavity-bound/pre-flight—rather than repeating v1's neonatal proportions. The full-body cavity view is explicitly a schematic cutaway, not a literal entrance-width claim.
- **Limitations:** One monitored regional nest; “resembled adults” is not a tract-by-tract plate; exact 24-day wing/tail lengths and sex unknown.

### HAR-BODRATI-ETAL-2024 — new primary distribution/habitat synthesis

- **Title:** Estatus de la Lechucita Canela (*Aegolius harrisii*) en el noreste argentino: distribución, vocalizaciones, comportamiento, hábitat y abundancia
- **Authors / journal:** Bodrati et al.; *Nuestras Aves* 69:67–86 (2024)
- **DOI / full PDF:** https://doi.org/10.56178/na.vi69.1049 · https://ri.conicet.gov.ar/bitstream/handle/11336/262582/CONICET_Digital_Nro.7163347f-2015-4004-a3a8-0bfb4ac8c7c5_B.pdf?isAllowed=y&sequence=2
- **Accessed/read:** 2026-10-29; pp.67–86, especially abstract pp.67–68, distribution pp.68–69 and discussion pp.82–83
- **Facts used:** 2003–2024 fieldwork added 36 northeastern-Argentina localities; *iheringi* is the northeastern form. In Misiones the species is often detected in degraded Atlantic Forest and appears rare in well-conserved forest.
- **Design boundary:** The exact 2019 nest's primary-forest setting remains appropriate for that nest; the 2024 synthesis prevents claiming that all birds require pristine forest.
- **Limitation:** Distribution/habitat synthesis, not juvenile plumage evidence.

### HAR-SIB-ARGENTINA-2026 — new official morphology/subspecies account

- **Institution / URL:** Argentina National Parks, Sistema de Información de Biodiversidad · https://sib.gob.ar/especies/aegolius-harrisii
- **Accessed:** 2026-10-29; taxonomy, description, distribution and habitat
- **Facts used:** *A. h. iheringi* in Misiones; small owl of forests, dark head, cinnamon face/forehead, black eye surrounds, paler cinnamon underparts, brown wings with white spots and dark tail with three pale/white spot rows.
- **Limitations:** Adult summary, not a growth series.

### HAR-MIK2013-447 — reused baseline morphology

- **Pages:** printed p.447 / PDF p.449 (previously read)
- **Use:** yellow eyes, no ear tufts, pale bluish-green/yellowish bill, black eye/ruff/chin pattern, plain ochre-buff underparts, bare pale-yellow toes; only modest female increase.
- **Boundary:** It did not describe natal down and is not used to override the 2019 exact-nest sequence.

**Source accounting:** new 2 / reused 2 / corrected 1. Exact individual age within the “24-day” observation, sex, tract sequence, size ratio and cutaway geometry remain unknown; science false.


## 2026-10-29 — #94 *Pulsatrix koeniswaldiana* v4 — southern-Brazil juvenile figure boundary

### KOE-GARCIA-ETAL-2015 — new primary regional record and juvenile figure

- **Title:** Registros de *Pulsatrix koeniswaldiana* em fragmentos de Mata Atlântica no Vale do Rio dos Sinos e primeiro espécime em coleção científica no Rio Grande do Sul
- **Authors / source:** Suélen de Almeida Garcia et al.; *Caderno de Pesquisa, série Biologia* 27(3):31–36 (2015)
- **DOI / URL:** https://doi.org/10.17058/cp.v27i3.6505 · https://seer.unisc.br/index.php/cadpesquisa/article/download/6505/4474
- **Accessed/read:** 2026-10-29; all pp.31–36; Fig.2 p.34 rendered and viewed
- **Taxon/stage:** exact species; juvenile recorded March 2013 in an Atlantic Forest fragment at Ivoti, Rio Grande do Sul
- **Facts:** Fig.2 shows a perched juvenile with pale fluffy body, dark facial mask, and partly developed short rounded wing; paper does not supply age, sex or motor score. Habitat is a highly fragmented semideciduous Atlantic Forest landscape.
- **Design decision:** use age-unknown post-cavity branch support and direct juvenile tonal/wing boundary; no adult-length wing, exposed long tail or flight claim.
- **Limitations:** single photographed juvenile; figure quality is low and does not resolve tract sequence or exact toe geometry.

### Reused sources

- **KOE-PEREIRA2013:** monitored Bauru pair used a natural top cavity in *Piptadenia gonoacantha*; young later perched outside. Supports post-cavity behavior, not exact age.
- **KOE-BOTTAI2013-Y:** dated young individual with dark mask, residual pale fluff and developed plumage; exact age/sex unknown.
- **KOE-MIK2013-313:** adult morphology baseline; female larger stated but no fixed ratio used.

**Source accounting:** new 1 / reused 3 / corrected 1. Exact age, sex, flight ability, wing/tail dimensions, toe geometry and pair ratio remain unknown; science false.


### MEL-ECUADOR-DIET-2022
- Title: What Do We Know about the Diet of Ecuadorian Owls?
- Authors/institution: Héctor Cadena-Ortiz et al.; INABIO and collaborators
- Year: 2022; DOI: https://doi.org/10.5772/intechopen.108594
- URL: https://www.intechopen.com/chapters/84796
- Accessed: 2026-10-29
- Type: peer-reviewed open-access review chapter with authors' field observation
- Taxon/stage: Pulsatrix melanota, recently fledged juvenile, Ecuador, July 2019
- Valuable fact: section 2.13 and Figure 2 document a recently fledged juvenile with a pellet; an adult remained close and defended it. This corroborates a supported post-fledging stage but does not provide exact age, wing dimensions or a flight threshold.
- Design use: supports labeling the central bird “recent fledgling — age unknown” and keeping an adult nearby; it does not license flight depiction or numerical development claims.
- Limits/confidence: single field observation; diet focus; moderate confidence for stage/context, low for detailed plumage beyond the figure.
- Links: species #95 v3; run 20261029T1702+0800-cycle-5; image version 95-pulsatrix-melanota-v3.


### RID-ITIS-2025
- Title/institution: ITIS Report — Aegolius ridgwayi; Integrated Taxonomic Information System
- Updated basis: IOC World Bird List v15.1 acquired 2025; accessed 2026-10-29
- URL: https://itis.gov/servlet/SingleRpt/SingleRpt?search_topic=TSN&search_value=686697
- Type: institutional nomenclatural database
- Taxon: Aegolius ridgwayi; all life stages
- Valuable fact: valid current species name is Aegolius ridgwayi (Alfaro, 1905); Cryptoglaux ridgwayi is the original/old combination. Historical ridgwayi, rostratus and tacanensis names are listed as synonyms in this treatment.
- Design use: keeps repository old-name crosswalk without splitting extra current species or transferring subspecies-specific juvenile detail.
- Limits/confidence: nomenclature only, not a morphology or development source; high confidence for name mapping.
- Links: species #96 v3; run 20261029T1731+0800-cycle-6.

### RID-AVES-CR-2025
- Title/institution: Lechucita parda — Aves de Costa Rica
- Author/site: Mauricio Calderón / Aves de Costa Rica; accessed 2026-10-29
- URL: https://www.avesdecostarica.com/Aegolius-ridgwayi.html
- Type: regional field-reference account with adult photograph; AI illustration explicitly excluded as evidence
- Taxon/stage: Aegolius ridgwayi, adult and juvenile summary
- Valuable fact: adults are small, large-headed and variably grey/earth brown with honey-yellow iris, dark horn bill and plain whitish-buff abdomen; juveniles resemble adults but are downier and may show slight breast streaking. Montane oak/cloud-forest edge habitat is described.
- Design use: supports a downier older juvenile with faint irregular breast streaking, not a white neonatal chick or adult-wing clone.
- Limits/confidence: no exact age, wing metric, flight threshold or nest structure; adult photo useful, AI illustration not used; moderate confidence.
- Links: species #96 v3; run 20261029T1731+0800-cycle-6.


## 2026-10-29 — #97 *Lophostrix cristata* v3 research refresh

- **CRI-SI-2014** — Smithsonian Research Online / STRI, “Opportunistic predation by Crested Owl *Lophostrix cristata* upon Seba's Short-tailed Bat,” Rocha & López-Baucells, 2014. https://repository.si.edu/items/79008db4-6fdd-40b2-ba21-56c288032f (accessed 2026-10-29). Institutional repository landing page; linked PDF and authors verified. Landing citation says 22(1):35–37, while the actual PDF prints pp.58–60. **Conflict correction:** use printed PDF pagination for page-specific evidence; do not silently merge both page ranges. Supports provenance and access, not additional morphology by itself. Related: #97 v3 run and CRI-ROCHA2014.
- **CRI-ROCHA2014-PDF** — Ricardo Rocha & Adrià López-Baucells, 2014, actual three-page article PDF, *Revista Brasileira de Ornitologia* 22(1), printed pp.58–60, https://mcng.cat/pdf/quiropters/Rocha-Lopez-Baucells-Revista-Brasileira-de-Ornitologia.pdf (accessed 2026-10-29). Full text read; printed p.59 Fig.1 caption identifies a juvenile captured in mature *Cecropia*-dominated secondary regrowth and flying on release. This supports a flight-capable older juvenile with developed wings and a secondary-forest setting, but not exact age, nest type, sex, or routine bat hunting. Primary field note; high confidence for the depicted individual, low generality.
- **CRI-MNHN-2010-261** — Muséum national d’Histoire naturelle, specimen MNHN-ZO-2010-261, https://science.mnhn.fr/institution/mnhn/collection/zo/item/2010-261 (accessed 2026-10-29). Museum specimen record: current name *Lophostrix cristata* (Daudin, 1800), female, age indeterminate, French Guiana; initial determination *Syrnium griseatum*. Supports old-name/current-name mapping and female provenance only. It cannot support juvenile stage or quantified sexual dimorphism because age and measurements are absent.
- **CRI-ONET-GALE144450** — Oiseaux.net, Anne & Gabriel Leboff, juvenile photo metadata gale144450, Panama, August 2013, https://www.oiseaux.net/en/photos/gabriel.leboff/crested.owl.4.html (accessed 2026-10-29). Actual metadata page read; supports existence of a field-photographed juvenile in Panama. Image is a northern population and metadata gives no age or measurements, so it is only a visual cross-check, not the nominate-Amazon plumage authority.

**Source-use summary:** new stable records 2 (CRI-SI-2014, CRI-MNHN-2010-261), reused 3 (CRI-ROCHA2014/PDF, CRI-MIK2013-358, CRI-ONET-GALE144450), corrected 1 (repository landing page-range conflict). The v3 design uses the documented older flight-capable stage and explicitly avoids inventing a nest or precise day age. Scientific verification remains false.


| MAG-ZAMBRANO-2022 | Brayan Zambrano, [Aves rapaces que nidifican en entornos urbanos y periurbanos de Santiago, Región Metropolitana, Chile central](https://aveschile.cl/wp-content/uploads/2023/06/3.-Aves-rapaces-que-nidifican-en-entornos-urbanos-y-periurbanos-de-Santiago-Chile-central.pdf) | 2022；访问2026-10-29 | 同行评审原始野外繁殖研究 | *Bubo magellanicus*；成年、约1周龄幼鸟、离巢前发育 | Revista Chilena de Ornitología 28(1):15–27；印刷pp.20–21，Fig.4 B–D（PDF pp.6–7） | 2015年圣地亚哥阳台巢的两只幼鸟约1周龄时仍有卵齿、眼睑已开；自动相机显示它们留巢直至飞羽（尾羽与飞羽）发育完成，约出生后50–56日离巢。Fig.4展示不同阶段和干植物层支撑。 | 单一城市繁殖对，不能代表普遍巢位或全部种群；没有给出逐日羽区测量、性别或首次飞行能力。本轮只将约1周龄卵齿/睁眼/低伏绒羽作为阶段边界，并把阳台明确标为有记录的个案背景。可信度：高。支持 #99 v3、运行记录 `owl-pet/production/runs/20261029T1830+0800-cycle-2.md`。 |
